| 44 | db | RETRIEVAL. With a single `referenced_paper_id`, `_retrieve_evidence` calls `get_paper_overview_context(paper_id, department, question)` — NO embedding, NO `match_chunks`, NO pgvector. It re-verifies ready+department, reads up to 200 chunk rows pinned to `active_index_version`, drops chunk_index 0 (cover page) unless nothing else survives, and returns a hardcoded similarity of 1.0. | `rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py:549` |
| 45 | service | For an inline reference (`is_overview_followup=False`) the question IS passed, so `rank_paper_chunks` scores chunks lexically: content-term hits capped at 4, section-title hits worth 3x capped at 2, plus +20 bonuses for literal 'objectives of the study' / 'scope and delimitation' / '3.2 methods'. Paper title and author words are excluded from the query terms. Top 5 become blocks [1]..[5]. For a whole-question numbered/ordinal reference the question is suppressed and the first 5 substantive chunks are used verbatim. | `rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py:514` |
| 46 | service | For the PLURAL path `referenced_paper_id` is a list: `asyncio.gather` runs one `get_paper_overview_context` per paper, then offsets each block's leading `[n]` marker and each source's `citation_id` by the running total. There is no cap on the number of papers. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:1919` |
| 47 | service | If resolution failed and no paper was pinned, the ordinary RAG path runs instead: `embed_text` -> `match_chunks` RPC at threshold 0.30 -> `rerank_candidates` (0.75 cosine + 0.25 normalised lexical) -> `apply_paper_diversity` -> exactly 5 blocks -> `long_context_reorder`, concurrently with `check_topic_duplication`. | `rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py:764` |
| 48 | service | Empty context returns the `NO_RELEVANT_PREFIX` message with `no_relevant_thesis=True`, `archive_current=True`, and any duplication alert attached. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2635` |
| 49 | external | GENERATION. Prompt selection: list -> `exact_papers_prompt`; overview follow-up -> `overview_prompt`; single pinned paper -> `exact_paper_prompt`; otherwise `grounded_prompt(department, question_type)`. A pinned paper's `generation_input` is ONLY `{context, question: effective_question}` — the conversation history is deliberately NOT sent. The unpinned path sends `{chat_history, context, question: req.question, resolved_question: effective_question}` and the human template tells the model to answer the Original Question using the resolved intent only to understand what it refers to. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2696` |
| 50 | service | POST-PROCESSING. `prompts.strip_no_evidence_sentinel` removes `ARCHIVE_COVERAGE_INSUFFICIENT` (flagging only a leading own-line occurrence, because the context is untrusted); `is_truncated(result)` reads the provider stop reason; `normalize_citation_markers` expands `[1, 2]` into `[1] [2]`. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2760` |
| 51 | service | For the plural path only, `_missing_referenced_papers` checks every pinned paper got a citation; if not, one bounded `_repair_multi_paper_coverage` rewrite runs (guest-charged but never refused). | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2772` |
| 52 | service | Citation ladder: `validate_citations` -> AI `_repair_citations` -> deterministic `enforce_citation_coverage` (which only assigns markers when every source is a chunk of ONE thesis — precisely the single-paper follow-up case) -> `_grounded_retrieval_fallback`. `filter_cited_sources` then drops every source the final answer did not cite. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2800` |
| 53 | service | `log_activity` is queued as a BackgroundTask with `sources_cited`, `citation_repaired`, `question_type`, `department`; fast paths queue a `fast_path` tag instead ('author_metadata', 'archive_inventory', 'query_translated', …). Failures are swallowed. | `rag-thesis-backend/services/activity.py:11` |
| 54 | service | `_chat_impl` stamps `kind` via `chat_notices.response_kind` (structural: `no_relevant_thesis` -> notice, `notice_type=='conversation'` -> notice, exact match against the constants, prefix match on NO_RELEVANT / GROUNDED_FALLBACK, else answer). | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2094` |
| 55 | api | PERSISTENCE (authenticated only). `_client_gone` polls Starlette's disconnect channel; if the reader pressed Stop or edited mid-flight the exchange is NOT saved. Otherwise, when `edit_from_turn` is set, `_truncate_session_from_turn` deletes the edited row and every later row (`.range(turn, turn+499)`), then `_persist_chat_exchange` runs. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2043` |
| 56 | db | `save_chat_exchange(p_user_id, p_session_id, p_title, p_question, p_answer, p_sources, p_duplication_alert, p_department, p_kind)` — SECURITY DEFINER, service_role only. It rejects a kind outside ('answer','notice'), creates the session if absent, re-verifies user AND department ownership, and inserts one `chat_messages` row holding the RAW user question, the answer, and the sources JSON. The old 8-arg signature was dropped so no caller can write an unmarked notice. | `rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:626` |
| 57 | frontend | The response is appended as an AI bubble; `res.kind` becomes `messageKind`, `session_id` is adopted if new (invalidating the sessions query), and `history_saved===false` raises a warning toast. `messageNoticeLabel` picks the chip: `no_relevant_thesis` -> 'Search completed · no qualifying archive evidence.', `messageKind==='notice'` -> 'System message · not a research answer.', `notice_type==='conversation'` -> no chip. | `rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Chat.jsx:1161` |

Those new `sources` become the next turn's `reference_sources` — the loop closes. | `rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Chat.jsx:726` |
| 59 | frontend | RELOAD PATH. `loadSession` GETs `/sessions/{id}/messages` (which returns `id,session_id,question,answer,sources,duplication_alert,kind,created_at` plus a server-derived `notice_type`) and rebuilds the transcript as alternating user/ai entries, carrying `messageKind` so a restored capacity apology is not mistaken for a research answer. Restored `sources` are what the next follow-up resolves against — via the database, not this array. | `rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Chat.jsx:1028` |

### Branches

- GUEST vs AUTHENTICATED reference source: guest -> `req.guest_source_ids` sent by the browser (Chat.jsx:1127), authenticated -> re-derived server-side from `chat_messages` (chat.py:2244). Both pass through `find_papers_by_ids`, so the client never controls which papers are readable.
- Author name vs positional reference: `try_author_fast_path` at chat.py:2422 runs BEFORE the numbered ladder, so a person name always wins. It runs a SECOND time at chat.py:2556 on the rewritten wording.
- Explicit author question vs soft follow-up: `who is X` / `sino si X Y` (>=2 surviving parts) gets a deterministic not-found notice on a miss; `what about X` gets nothing and falls through (chat.py:2405, chat.py:1222).
- One-word author token vs multi-part: one word requires `_author_token_matches` against whole archived name parts and returns up to 5; multi-part uses an ILIKE on the full string with a surname fallback filtered by `_author_name_matches` (retriever.py:163).
- Whole-question positional (`number 2`, `the second thesis`) -> OVERVIEW of that paper, `is_overview_followup=True`, question suppressed in ranking, `overview_prompt`. Inline positional (`what are the objectives of number 2`) -> SPECIFIC question, `is_overview_followup=False`, the numeral is textually replaced by the title, `exact_paper_prompt`.
- `_IN_DOCUMENT_COUNTABLE` veto: 'objective number 2' / 'chapter no. 3' are positions INSIDE a manuscript, not in the prior answer, so both the inline resolver (chat.py:1697) and the listed-item guard (chat.py:975) reject them.
- Plural reference (`their`, `both theses`, `summarize them`) with >=2 reference sources -> ALL of them are pinned, `exact_papers_prompt`, and a coverage repair runs if any paper goes uncited.
- Generic ambiguous follow-up with >=2 sources -> pin all; with exactly 1 -> pin it and prefix the question; with 0 -> pay for a Gemini rewrite (guest-refusable) or the deterministic string fallback.
- Explicit quoted title present but NOT uniquely resolved -> `elif not explicit_title and is_ambiguous_followup(...)` means the generic follow-up branch is skipped entirely and the turn goes to plain semantic search.
- Title fragment resolves to exactly one ready paper -> treated identically to 'number 2'. Zero, two-plus, or a saturated 25-row candidate page -> treated as if the fragment never existed.
- Archive listing continuation vs item reference: `_references_a_listed_item` (chat.py:973) vetoes the paging branch whenever an inline number, an inline ordinal, or 'about it/this/that/the thesis' is present, so 'tell me more about number 3' reaches the resolver while 'show me the rest' pages the catalog.
- Weak continuation phrasings ('more', 'continue', 'ano pa', 'iba pa') are only accepted when `_last_turn_was_a_listing` — i.e. the answer directly above contains the marker 'from the live indexed archive'.
- `not reference_sources` gates the ambiguous system-identity and system-origin fast paths: with an archived source in play 'who developed this system' stays a retrieval question. `_SELF_PLATFORM_REFERENCE` ('itong system na ginagamit ko') is deliberately UNGATED so a correction survives the wrong answer's sources.
- Filipino/Ilocano vs English follow-up detection: English signals stand alone; every local signal is gated behind `_names_its_own_subject`, whose acronym/genitive/topic/year/scope vetoes are what keep 'ano ang layunin ng SECURE?' from being pinned despite being under five words.
- Non-English resolved question -> one extra Gemini call to produce an English retrieval query (guest-refusable); the generation prompt still receives the original wording.
- Capacity cooldown active -> every question returns HTTP 200 with CAPACITY_MESSAGE, but only AFTER the author fast path has had its chance (chat.py:2427).
- Prompt edit (`edit_from_turn`) -> history is read via `_history_before_turn` against the full ordered transcript, and the old branch is deleted only after the new answer succeeds.
- `_client_gone` true -> the answer is returned to a socket nobody is reading and the exchange is never persisted, so the next follow-up resolves against the turn before it.

### Failure modes

- No prior sources at all (fresh session, or the previous turn was a notice): `_resolve_numbered_thesis_reference` returns None at chat.py:1670. 'tell me about number 2' then also fails `is_ambiguous_followup` (5 words, no '?'), so the literal string 'tell me about number 2' is embedded and searched. Concrete result: HTTP 200, `no_relevant_thesis=true`, kind=notice, answer = 'No relevant thesis was found in the CCSICT archive for that query. Try rephrasing with different technical terms, or ask about another topic.', sources=[], UI chip 'Search completed · no qualifying archive evidence.' The user is never told the reference could not be resolved.
- Position out of range ('number 11' against 3 sources): same silent path. Verified: `_resolve_numbered_thesis_reference` -> None, `_resolve_inline_thesis_reference` -> None, `is_ambiguous_followup('number 11', prior)` -> False. Literal semantic search, same no-relevant notice.
- Same question WITH a question mark takes a different route: 'what about number 2?' passes `is_ambiguous_followup` (<=5 words + '?') so with no reference sources it spends a full Gemini rewrite call; without one it does not. Same input, different cost and different answer, decided by punctuation.
- Referenced paper no longer ready / moved department: `find_papers_by_ids` (retriever.py:185) silently drops it and COMPACTS the list, so every later position shifts by one and 'number 3' answers about what used to be number 4. No warning anywhere.
- Referenced paper still ready but its `active_index_version` has no chunks: `get_paper_overview_context` returns ('', [], 0.0) (retriever.py:634), `context` is falsy, and chat.py:2635 returns the generic no-relevant notice with `archive_current=True` — indistinguishable from 'the archive has nothing on this'.
- Author lookup miss on an explicit question: HTTP 200, kind='answer' (not notice), answer = 'I could not verify <Name> as an author in the selected ISU thesis archive. Try the complete name or ask for a specific thesis title.', sources=[], `archive_current=True`.
- Author metadata query fails after `retry_transient` exhausts: HTTPException 503 'The thesis archive is temporarily unavailable. Please try again in a moment.' (chat.py:2398). The frontend catches it, removes the optimistic bubble, restores the question into the composer, and shows the 'IskAI could not answer' error card plus a toast (Chat.jsx:1175).
- `find_papers_by_ids` failure on the AUTHENTICATED path is NOT caught (chat.py:2252) — the exception escapes `_chat_impl_unstamped` and becomes an unhandled 500. The GUEST path at chat.py:2264 wraps the identical call in try/except and degrades to no references. Same operation, two different outcomes.
- Exact title lookup failure: 503 with the archive-unavailable detail (chat.py:2444). Title FRAGMENT lookup failure: logged at warning and treated as no match (chat.py:2464), because a fragment is a guess rather than a stated reference.
- Follow-up rewrite Gemini call fails or returns multi-line / >1000 chars / 'answer:'-prefixed text: caught at chat.py:1810, logged, and replaced by `fallback_standalone_question`, which embeds the literal two-line string 'Previous research question: … Follow-up: …'.
- Guest allowance exhausted at the rewrite step: `_guest_budget_response` -> HTTP 200 with `GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE`, sources=[], kind=notice, and an activity row tagged stage='followup_rewrite'. The same refusal can fire at stage='pre_retrieval', 'query_translation' and 'pre_generation'.
- Provider capacity error during retrieval or generation: `mark_capacity_limited()` trips a 60 s process-wide cooldown and the turn returns HTTP 200 with CAPACITY_MESSAGE (chat.py:2626, chat.py:2735) — never a 429/503.
- Any other retrieval exception: 503 'The thesis archive is temporarily unavailable…'. Any other generation exception: 502 'The research AI service is temporarily unavailable. Please try again later.'
- Generation truncated at the output ceiling: the draft is DISCARDED, not repaired — `_grounded_retrieval_fallback` serves 'I could not verify a direct answer from the retrieved thesis text. The closest archived studies are …' which `response_kind` classifies as a notice (chat.py:2801).
- Model reports no evidence (sentinel or phrase list): the repair ladder is deliberately bypassed; cited sources are kept if any survive, otherwise the generic no-relevant notice with `no_relevant_thesis=True` (chat.py:2818).
- Citation repair fails twice: `_grounded_retrieval_fallback` again (chat.py:2876).
- Session not owned / wrong department: 404 'Session not found' from `_ensure_session_owner`, or 409 'This conversation belongs to a different department. Start a new conversation.' (chat.py:1524).
- Guest sends `session_id`: 401 'Guest conversations do not have saved sessions.' (chat.py:2117).
- Persistence fails after a good answer: caught at chat.py:2062, `history_saved=false`, the answer still renders, and the frontend shows 'Answer received, but chat history was not saved'. If this was the FIRST turn, no `session_id` is returned, so the NEXT follow-up has no history and no reference sources at all.
- Reader presses Stop or edits mid-flight: `_client_gone` returns True, the exchange is not persisted, the browser drops the optimistic bubble via `dropPendingPrompt`, and the follow-up chain silently loses a turn.

### What gets persisted

- `chat_messages` row per exchange, written by the `save_chat_exchange` RPC (rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:626): `session_id, question (RAW user wording, never the resolved one), answer, sources (jsonb), duplication_alert, kind ('answer'|'notice'), created_at`. The `sources` array on this row is the sole durable input to the next turn's positional resolution.
- `chat_sessions` row, created lazily inside the same RPC when `p_session_id` is null, titled from the first 40 chars of the question and stamped with the resolved department.
- `chat_messages` DELETEs on a prompt edit: `_truncate_session_from_turn` (chat.py:1530) removes the edited row and every later one via `.range(turn, turn+499)` — executed only after the replacement answer succeeded.
- `activity_log` row per turn via BackgroundTask (services/activity.py:11): `user_id` (null for guests), `action` ('chat_query' | 'chat_query_blocked' | 'chat_query_budget_exhausted'), `department`, and a `detail` jsonb carrying `question_length`, `sources_cited`, `duplication_flagged`, `citation_repaired`, `question_type`, and for fast paths a `fast_path` tag ('author_metadata', 'archive_inventory', 'system_origin', 'query_translated', 'unclear_topic', …). Insert failures are swallowed.
- Guest token spend increments in the shared `guest_budget` store, once before generation and again for each extra model call (rewrite, translation, citation repair, coverage repair, duplication summary).
- Nothing else: the resolved/rewritten question, the retrieved context, the per-chunk evidence text and the `reference_sources` array are all in-process only. `ChatResponse.sources` is metadata-only (`retriever.public_source`, retriever.py:44) — no PDF URLs, no storage paths, no chunk content.
- Browser-side: nothing is written to localStorage for the transcript. The React `messages` array is the only client copy and is rebuilt from `/sessions/{id}/messages` on reload.

### Surprises

- THE CITATION NUMBER THE READER SEES IS NOT THE INDEX THE SERVER USES. `reference_sources` is built by deduplicating the prior answer's sources BY PAPER ID (chat.py:2248), but the grounded path emits up to `retrieval_per_paper_cap=3` chunks from the SAME paper, each with its own citation_id (retriever.py:1008). Verified concretely: sources with citation_ids [1,2,3,4,5] over papers [A,A,A,B,C] collapse to ['A','B','C'], so 'number 2' resolves to B (the reader's [4]), 'number 3' resolves to C (the reader's [5]), and 'number 4'/'number 5' resolve to nothing at all and fall through to a literal semantic search. This is only correct for archive listings and author lookups, where sources are one-per-paper. No test covers the duplicated-paper case.
- The comment at chat.py:2241 says positional references must mean 'the immediately preceding answer', but the code at chat.py:2244 is a reverse `next()` that walks back to the most recent turn that HAS sources — skipping over any number of intervening sourceless answers. The test named `test_numbered_session_followup_ignores_sources_from_older_answers` only proves the newest-with-sources wins, not that the immediately preceding turn does.
- A resolved numbered/ordinal/title follow-up NEVER touches pgvector. `_retrieve_evidence` short-circuits to `get_paper_overview_context`, which is plain PostgREST table reads, and returns a hardcoded `top_similarity` of 1.0 (retriever.py:670). The 0.30 threshold, `match_chunks`, the hybrid rerank and `long_context_reorder` are all bypassed, yet the response still sets `archive_current=True` and the UI still prints 'Searched the current indexed archive'.
- A pinned-paper follow-up also gets NO duplication check: `_retrieve_evidence` returns `alert=None` for every `referenced_paper_id` branch (chat.py:1944, chat.py:1955), so the query-time 85% novelty guard is silently skipped on exactly the turns where the user is drilling into one thesis.
- The 'exactly 5 context blocks' invariant does not hold on the plural path. `_retrieve_evidence` gathers one overview per pinned paper with no cap, each returning up to 5 chunks (chat.py:1919). `find_papers_by_ids` caps references at 10, so a plural follow-up can ship 50 context blocks and 50 sources.
- The author fast path runs on the RAW question before the positional ladder, and `_extract_author_name` will happily read 'what about second thesis' (no article) as the person name 'Second Thesis' — verified. It is saved only by the archive not containing an author whose name matches, and it still costs a database round trip on a purely ordinal reference. 'what about THE second thesis' is blocked, because 'the' is in the leading-word reject list.
- A deterministic author MISS is persisted as `kind='answer'`, not as a notice. 'I could not verify <Name> as an author…' is not in `NOTICE_MARKERS` and `response_kind` does not recognise it, so it becomes conversational context replayed to the model on the next turn — the exact failure mode the B14 migration exists to prevent.
- The multi-match author answer numbers its entries [1], [2], [3] in text, but `find_papers_by_author` builds sources through `public_source(paper)` with NO `citation_id` (retriever.py:180). The UI's `citationIds = sources.map((item, itemIndex) => item.citation_id ?? itemIndex + 1)` computes the index WITHIN each per-paper group (Chat.jsx:300), and every group has exactly one entry — so every source card shows the chip [1] while the prose says [1], [2], [3].
- Follow-up detection is partly case-sensitive in a way that breaks ALL-CAPS input. `_FOLLOWUP_REFERENCE` uses `(?-i:it|its)` so 'IT' is not a pronoun (deliberate — BSIT), but `_names_its_own_subject`'s acronym pattern then treats any all-caps word as a named subject. Verified: 'SUMMARIZE IT' and 'summarize THEM' are NOT follow-ups, while 'Summarize it' is.
- Punctuation decides whether a turn costs a Gemini call. `is_ambiguous_followup` ends with `words <= 5 and normalized.endswith('?')` (guards.py:599), so 'what about number 2?' enters the rewrite branch and 'tell me about number 2' does not.
- A quoted title that fails to resolve uniquely disables the whole generic follow-up branch: `elif not explicit_title and is_ambiguous_followup(...)` (chat.py:2505). A user who quotes a title with a typo loses both the exact path AND every pronoun resolution for that turn.
- Title-fragment resolution is checked BEFORE the numbered reference (chat.py:2483 vs chat.py:2489), so a fragment that happens to match exactly one paper outranks an explicit 'number 2' in the same sentence.
- `_history_before_turn` and `_truncate_session_from_turn` treat `edit_from_turn` as an index into the full `chat_messages` row sequence, while the browser computes it as the count of USER bubbles before the edited prompt (transcript.js:38). These agree only because every stored row contributes exactly one user bubble — which holds only as long as `_client_gone` keeps unsent turns out of the table.
- The `kind` backfill in migrations/20260804_chat_message_kind.sql:43 lists four notice prefixes, and `LEGACY_CONVERSATION_PREFIX` ("Hello! I'm IskAI, the research assistant for the ISU Thesis AI Library.") is not one of them. `is_stored_non_answer` also misses it, because it prefix-matches the CURRENT greeting wordings. Legacy greeting rows therefore stay `kind='answer'` and are still replayed to the model as context — `notice_type()` recognises them for rendering but nothing filters them.
- The deterministic rewrite fallback embeds a literal labelled string. `fallback_standalone_question` returns 'Previous research question: <prev> Follow-up: <q>' (guards.py:602), and that whole two-line text — labels included — is what `embed_text` receives when the rewrite call fails.
- The generation prompt for a pinned paper deliberately receives NO conversation history: `generation_input` is `{context, question}` only when `referenced_paper_id` is truthy (chat.py:2704). All the follow-up context lives in the rewritten question string, not in the prompt's history slot.
- `_is_plural_source_followup` fires on a bare 'give me a summary' or 'summarize' with >=2 remembered papers (chat.py:1739) — no pronoun and no plural noun required — which turns an innocuous request into a multi-paper fan-out.
- An archive COUNT answer contains the listing marker 'from the live indexed archive' but ships `sources=[]`, so `_last_turn_was_a_listing` says yes while `_archive_listing_cursor` stays 0. The result is that 'show me the rest' after a bare count re-lists page one rather than continuing, which happens to be right but for a reason nothing states.
- `_FIL_ENCLITIC_POSSESSOR` is positional, not lexical: the discriminator between the Ilocano possessor `da` and the plural personal article `da` is a closed aspect noun immediately to the left and nothing resolvable to the right (guards.py:458). Both readings appear in the single 2026-09-14 transcript the whole multilingual layer was built from, and `tests/test_followup_multilingual.py` pins 172 strings on this one distinction.

**Adversarial fact-check:** verdict `mostly-accurate`, 11 correction(s), 7 omission(s). Corrections are already folded into the text above. They are kept because each records a wrong belief a competent reader reached from the same files - the same trap is available to you.

- **Wrong:** Step 6/8/9: route POST /chat at rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:1985; department resolved once at chat.py:2007; edit_from_turn 400 at chat.py:2015.
  **Actual:** All three anchors are ~20-25 lines early. `@router.post('')` is at chat.py:2007 and `async def chat` at chat.py:2010; `ensure_guest_chat_verification` is chat.py:2016; the single `resolve_effective_department` call is chat.py:2027-2029; the `A prompt edit requires a saved session.` 400 is chat.py:2035-2038. chat.py:1985 is `_invoke_generation`. The described behaviour and order are correct — only the anchors are wrong. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2007`)
- **Wrong:** Surprise #1: the grounded path emits up to retrieval_per_paper_cap=3 chunks per paper, each with its own citation_id (retriever.py:1008).
  **Actual:** The mechanism is correct and verified, but retriever.py has 984 lines — retriever.py:1008 does not exist. The real anchors are `cited_chunks = [{**chunk, 'citation_id': index + 1} ...]` at services/retriever.py:868 (citation ids assigned per chunk, pre-LongContextReorder) and `retrieval_per_paper_cap: int = Field(default=3, ge=1, le=5)` at config.py:89. The dedupe-by-paper-id at chat.py:2248 is confirmed, so the [1..5]→3-paper collapse is real. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py:868`)
- **Wrong:** Surprise: `_is_plural_source_followup` fires on a bare 'give me a summary' or 'summarize' with >=2 remembered papers (chat.py:1739).
  **Actual:** Half wrong. The third alternative is `\b(?:provide|give|show|tell me|what is|summari[sz]e)\s+(?:me\s+)?(?:a\s+|the\s+)?(?:summary|summaries)\b` — the noun `summary`/`summaries` is mandatory. A bare 'summarize' does NOT fire this branch; 'summarize them' fires only via the separate `\b(?:their|them)\b` alternative at chat.py:1738. 'give me a summary' does fire, as claimed. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:1739`)
- **Wrong:** Surprise #18: after a bare archive COUNT answer, 'show me the rest' re-lists page one rather than continuing.
  **Actual:** Verified empirically and it is wrong. With cursor 0, `continues_listing = bool(listing_cursor) and ...` is False (chat.py:2282), and `_is_archive_inventory_question('show me the rest', ...)` is also False, so the inventory branch is not entered at all — the turn falls through to the ordinary pipeline/semantic search, not to page one. (`_is_archive_continuation_question` alone returns True, but it is AND-ed with the cursor.) The rest of the surprise holds: the count answer does contain 'from the live indexed archive', `_last_turn_was_a_listing` returns True, and `_archive_listing_cursor` returns 0. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2281`)
- **Wrong:** Step 57: `messageNoticeLabel` picks the chip: no_relevant_thesis -> 'Search completed…', messageKind==='notice' -> 'System message…', notice_type==='conversation' -> no chip.
  **Actual:** The order is inverted and the order is load-bearing. The real sequence is `no_relevant_thesis` -> NO_EVIDENCE_LABEL, then `notice_type === 'conversation'` -> null, then `messageKind === 'notice'` -> SYSTEM_NOTICE_LABEL. A conversational notice also carries kind='notice', so if the conversation test ran last (as the trace orders it) every greeting would get the 'System message · not a research answer.' chip. (`rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/chat/messageNotice.js:31`)
- **Wrong:** Step 5: 'the axios interceptor attaches the Supabase JWT or X-Guest-ID and, for guests, X-Turnstile-Token.'
  **Actual:** `X-Turnstile-Token` is not set by the interceptor. `chatQuery` sets it as a per-call header on the POST itself (api.js:191); the request interceptor (api.js:72-90) attaches only the JWT / X-Guest-ID. Also relevant and omitted: the response interceptor retries ONLY `config.method === 'get'` on 502/504 (api.js:116, TRANSIENT_GATEWAY_STATUSES at api.js:17), so a chat POST that returns 502 is never retried. chatQuery's own anchor api.js:167 and the exact payload field list are correct. (`rag-thesis-frontend/src/api.js:191`)
- **Wrong:** Surprise: '`_FOLLOWUP_REFERENCE` uses `(?-i:it|its)` so IT is not a pronoun, but `_names_its_own_subject`'s acronym pattern then treats any all-caps word as a named subject.'
  **Actual:** The conclusion is verified empirically ('summarize it' True, 'SUMMARIZE IT' False, 'summarize THEM' False, 'summarize them' True) but the explanation is off: `_FOLLOWUP_REFERENCE` is compiled with NO `re.IGNORECASE` flag at all, so the whole pattern — including `they|them|their|these|those` — is case-sensitive. The `(?-i:…)` group is redundant defensive scoping. 'THEM' fails on the regex before the acronym veto is ever consulted. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/guards.py:280`)
- **Wrong:** Step 32: "'the second thesis' (-> 'second', 1 token)" falls through the fragment path.
  **Actual:** 'the second thesis' never produces a fragment: `_extract_thesis_title_fragment` requires a `what/how about` or `tell me (more) about` opener (`_LOOSE_TITLE_REFERENCE`, chat.py:236), and returns None here. It is resolved by the whole-question `_ORDINAL_THESIS_REFERENCE` instead. The '-> second, 1 token' description fits 'what about the second thesis', not 'the second thesis'. Everything else in step 32 is exact (3 tokens / 12 chars / 4-char lookup token / 25-row saturation, retriever.py:295-300, 319). (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:1770`)
- **Wrong:** Step 12: fast-path gauntlet uses `_short_query_candidates`, chat.py:690.
  **Actual:** `_short_query_candidates` is defined at chat.py:638; chat.py:690 is inside `_is_simple_conversation` (def at 688). The described contents are right: 80-char cap for greeting (chat.py:695) and capability (chat.py:768), 40-char cap for courtesy (chat.py:784) and farewell (chat.py:798), and the gauntlet order model→capability→courtesy/farewell→greeting/identity→self-origin matches chat.py:2125/2142/2155/2173/2189 exactly. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:638`)
- **Wrong:** Scattered anchors in the post-retrieval section: step 48 'empty context' at chat.py:2635; capacity mark during retrieval at chat.py:2626; truncation fallback at chat.py:2801; no-evidence branch at chat.py:2818; second citation-repair fallback at chat.py:2876; `_retrieve_evidence` returns alert=None at chat.py:1944/1955; `get_paper_overview_context` empty return at retriever.py:634; inline-resolver veto at chat.py:1697.
  **Actual:** Each is 2-10 lines early and lands on a comment or an adjacent line. Actual: `if not context:` chat.py:2645; retrieval `_mark_capacity_limited()` chat.py:2631; truncation `_grounded_retrieval_fallback` chat.py:2811; `elif reports_no_evidence:` chat.py:2813; final repair fallback chat.py:2880; `_retrieve_evidence` returns chat.py:1946 and chat.py:1956; overview empty returns retriever.py:588 and retriever.py:632; the `_IN_DOCUMENT_COUNTABLE` veto chat.py:1700. All the described behaviours are correct. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2645`)
- **Wrong:** Step 41: 'The rewritten/resolved question is re-run through prohibited_reason' presented as a pipeline step following the ladder.
  **Actual:** Anchor chat.py:2546 is exact, but the re-check is scoped INSIDE the `elif not explicit_title and is_ambiguous_followup(...)` branch (8-space indent under chat.py:2505). The fragment, numbered, inline and plural branches all rewrite `effective_question` and are never re-screened. The second author fast-path attempt at chat.py:2556-2557 is correctly described as unscoped (`if effective_question != req.question:`). (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2546`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- POST-RETRIEVAL GATE THE TRACE HAS NO STEP FOR: `_is_unsupported_single_token_query(req.question, context, sources)` at rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2659 runs between the empty-context return and the guest generation charge. It returns `chat_notices.UNCLEAR_TOPIC_MESSAGE` with an explicit `kind='notice'` (the only place in the pipeline that sets kind inline rather than letting `_chat_impl` stamp it) and logs `fast_path='unclear_topic'`. A one-token follow-up that survived resolution can die here.
- SECOND UNTRACED POST-GENERATION GUARD: `_looks_like_misdirected_greeting(answer)` at chat.py:2792 (predicate at chat.py:1290) replaces the whole draft with `_grounded_retrieval_fallback` when the model opens 'hello/hi /hey ' AND contains "i'm iskai"/'i am iskai'/'iskai here'. This runs before the citation ladder and reclassifies the turn as a notice.
- REQUEST-MODEL VALIDATION LAYER: `ChatRequest` in rag-thesis-backend/models.py:15-44 enforces question 1-4000 chars, `guest_history` max 5 items (each <=4000), `guest_source_ids` max 10 items (each <=64 chars), `edit_from_turn >= 0`, `thesis_category_filter` pattern `^(student|faculty)$`, and `extra='ignore'`. A guest posting 11 source ids gets a 422 before the route body — the trace attributes the 10-cap solely to `find_papers_by_ids`/`source_ids[:10]`.
- DOUBLE OWNERSHIP ROUND TRIP: `_ensure_session_owner` already queried `chat_sessions` at chat.py:2118-2123, and `_load_chat_history` (chat.py:1492-1496) issues its OWN `chat_sessions` ownership select and 404 before reading history. Every authenticated turn with a session pays two ownership queries, with two different error contracts (404 only in the loader, 404+409 in the guard).
- AUTHENTICATED FIRST TURN HAS NO REFERENCE SOURCES BY CONSTRUCTION: the history branch is `if req.session_id:` / `elif not user:` (chat.py:2229/2257), not `if user:`. An authenticated user whose first turn has not yet returned a session_id gets `reference_sources = []` — which is also the mechanism behind the trace's own 'persistence fails on the FIRST turn' failure mode, but it applies to every new conversation's second message too if the first turn was never persisted.
- RESTORED TRANSCRIPTS LOSE TWO RESPONSE FIELDS: `loadSession` (Chat.jsx:1049-1067) rebuilds AI entries from `answer, sources, duplication_alert, messageKind, notice_type, created_at` only. `no_relevant_thesis` and `archive_current` are not columns and are not reconstructed, so after a reload a no-evidence notice downgrades from the specific 'Search completed · no qualifying archive evidence.' chip to the generic system-notice chip, and the 'Searched the current indexed archive' line disappears from every restored answer.
- `rank_paper_chunks` short-circuits twice (services/retriever.py:516 and 542): it returns the chunks UNRANKED when every query term is also a paper title/author term, and again when every chunk scores 0. So an inline reference like 'what are the methods of number 2' can silently fall back to chunk_index order rather than lexical rank — the trace presents ranking as unconditional.

## 4. Refusal, notice and capacity-degradation paths

<a id="4-refusals"></a>

Flow key: `refusals`

### Overview

Refusal, notice and capacity-degradation paths in POST /chat — every non-answer outcome from browser keystroke to persisted row and rendered chip

**Trigger:** A user (guest or signed-in) submits a message in the composer on /chat. `Chat.jsx::send` (rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Chat.jsx:1115) appends an optimistic user bubble and calls `chatQuery` (rag-thesis-frontend/src/api.js:167), which POSTs to /chat. Every outcome below is produced inside one request; nine of them never call Gemini at all.

**Actors, in order:** Chat.jsx (composer, transcript, AiBubble) -> useGuestChatGate.js (Turnstile arming) -> api.js axios instance + response interceptor -> routers/chat.py::chat (route wrapper) -> dependencies/auth.py::require_chat_access / get_optional_user -> services/turnstile.py::ensure_guest_chat_verification -> routers/chat.py::_chat_impl / _chat_impl_unstamped -> services/guards.py::prohibited_reason -> services/guest_budget.py -> services/chat_notices.py (capacity cooldown + notice classifier) -> services/retriever.py::search_chunks -> services/gemini_pool.py::arun (gateway -> primary -> reserves) -> services/prompts.py (NO_EVIDENCE_SENTINEL) -> services/llm_output.py::is_truncated -> services/citations.py -> Postgres RPC save_chat_exchange -> routers/sessions.py::get_session_messages (reload path) -> pages/chat/messageNotice.js (chip)

**Latency:** "The five conversational fast paths and the two provenance paths are pure string work — regex fullmatch and set membership over at most three normalized candidate strings — so they return in microseconds plus one BackgroundTask enqueue; no embedding, no RPC, no Gemini. The refusal path is two regex searches. The capacity cooldown short-circuit is the measured 1-18 ms band the JMeter summarizer keys on (summarize_chat_load.py:16-19), but note it sits AFTER the session-owner check, the history load and the guest-budget read, so an authenticated caller still pays 2-3 Supabase round trips before receiving it — only a bare guest (no session_id, no guest_source_ids) short-circuits `find_papers_by_ids` at retriever.py:188 and `is_exhausted` at guest_budget.py:125 and hits the sub-20 ms path. The no-results notice costs one embedding + one `match_chunks` RPC + a concurrent `check_topic_duplication` and no generation. The UNCLEAR_TOPIC notice costs the same. The truncation fallback is the most expensive non-answer: a full generation that is then thrown away, and on the gateway route that generation can spend nearly its whole 6000-token ceiling reasoning (the measured case: 1,920 of 1,996 tokens). Genuine answers were measured at 5.1-26.2 s against a 100 ms / 2000 ms banding, so the gap between a notice and an answer is wide and unambiguous. The gateway hop is skipped entirely while cooling rather than merely demoted, specifically because an unreachable gateway can hang for `gemini_timeout_seconds` and would otherwise add a minute to every question (gemini_pool.py:222-228)."

### Steps (44)

| # | Layer | What happens | Anchor |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | frontend | `send` checks `guestGate.isReady()`. If Turnstile is enabled and no token is held, the send is PARKED: the question goes back into the composer, `awaitingCheck` is set, nothing is sent. No network call, no notice. | `rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Chat.jsx:1102-1109` |
| 2 | frontend | Otherwise the request body is assembled: `guest_history` (last 5 user texts, guests only), `guest_source_ids` (last AI message's source ids, deduped, max 10), and `conversation_replies` — the answers of prior messages whose `notice_type === 'conversation'`, last 30. That last field exists ONLY to rotate the greeting/thanks/farewell variants. | `rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Chat.jsx:1121-1151` |
| 3 | api | slowapi applies two limiters on the route: per-IP (`ip_rate_limit_key`) and per-key. Exceeding either raises `RateLimitExceeded`, handled by slowapi's own handler registered in main.py — a raw 429 with slowapi's body, NOT a ChatResponse and NOT the capacity notice. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2008-2009` |
| 4 | api | `require_chat_access` runs. `get_optional_user` validates the JWT with Supabase; an invalid supplied token is 401 `Invalid or expired authentication token` (never silently downgraded to guest). `_ensure_approved_account` gives 403 `This account is pending approval or has been rejected.` for a non-approved profile, or 503 `Account validation is temporarily unavailable.` if the profiles read fails. | `rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py:243-260, 141-155` |
| 5 | api | `_require_feature(user,'chat',...)` — a signed-in role without the `chat` feature gets 403 `Research chat is not enabled for your role.`. Admin/superadmin short-circuit to allowed. **`_require_privileged_mfa` is deliberately NOT called here** (explicit comment), so the privileged-MFA refusal is structurally unreachable on POST /chat. | `rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py:384-409` |
| 6 | api | `ensure_guest_chat_verification`. Guests only, and only when `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` is set. Missing guest id or token -> 403 detail `Please complete the security check to continue as a guest.` with header `X-Guest-Verification: required`. A token Cloudflare rejects (or any verify error — fail closed) -> 403 `Security check failed or expired. Please try the check again.` with `X-Guest-Verification: failed`. | `rag-thesis-backend/services/turnstile.py:111-137` |
| 7 | api | `resolve_effective_department`: guest -> `THESIS_EVALUATION_DEPARTMENT`; non-superadmin requesting another department -> 403 `You can only search your assigned department.`; superadmin naming an unknown department -> 422 `Unknown department.`; departments table unreachable -> 503 `Department validation is unavailable.` | `rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py:105-129` |
| 8 | api | `edit_from_turn` without `session_id` -> 400 `A prompt edit requires a saved session.` Checked before generation so a malformed edit does not burn a model call. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2035-2038` |
| 9 | service | `_chat_impl` delegates to `_chat_impl_unstamped`, then unconditionally overwrites `response.kind = chat_notices.response_kind(response)`. This one line is why all ~14 return statements below share a classification with persistence. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2091-2095` |
| 10 | service | If `session_id` is set and the caller is a guest -> 401 `Guest conversations do not have saved sessions.` Then `_ensure_session_owner`: 404 `Session not found`, or 409 `This conversation belongs to a different department. Start a new conversation.` | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2115-2123, 1511-1527` |
| 11 | service | FAST PATH 1 — model identity. `_is_model_question` = exact membership of the fully normalized (`[^a-z0-9 ]+`->space, whitespace collapsed) message in `_MODEL_QUESTIONS` (8 phrases, e.g. 'what model are you'). Returns `ChatResponse(answer=_model_response(), sources=[], notice_type='conversation')`. The text is dynamic: 'IskAI uses gemini-3.6-flash to generate citation-backed answers and Gemini Embedding to find relevant thesis evidence. It answers only from the indexed CCSICT thesis archive.' Activity `chat_query` fast_path=`model_identity`. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2126-2138, 801-803, 854-859` |
| 12 | service | FAST PATH 2 — capabilities. `_is_capability_question`: `_short_query_candidates` produces up to 3 readings (as typed / politeness particles `po`,`ho` removed / trailing addressee stripped), rejects if the AS-TYPED reading exceeds 80 chars, then exact membership in `_CAPABILITY_QUESTIONS_ALL` (10 English + 27 Filipino/Ilocano), optionally after any greeting prefix. Returns `CAPABILITIES_MESSAGE` verbatim, sources=[], notice_type='conversation'. Deliberately ordered BEFORE the greeting so 'what can you do' is not answered with an introduction. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2142-2154, 765-779, 638-667` |
| 13 | service | FAST PATH 3 — thanks / farewell. `_is_courtesy_message`: same candidate expansion, cap of 40 chars on the as-typed reading, exact membership in `_THANKS_ALL` (13 English + 24 local) or `_FAREWELLS_ALL` (9 + 16). `_is_farewell_message` re-tests the farewell set only to pick the pool. `chat_notices.varied_message` then picks the least-recently-used variant from the 10-entry pool using `req.conversation_replies` — deterministic, no repeat until the pool is exhausted. Defaults are 'You're welcome! Ask me anytime about archived thesis topics, methods, findings, or authors.' and 'Goodbye! Your saved conversation will be here when you return.' notice_type='conversation'; fast_path=`thanks`\|`farewell`. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2156-2173, 782-798; rag-thesis-backend/services/chat_notices.py:114-157` |
| 14 | service | FAST PATH 4 — greeting / identity. `_is_simple_conversation`: 80-char cap, then (a) whole-message membership in `_GREETINGS_ALL` or `_IDENTITY_QUESTIONS_ALL`, or fullmatch of the two time-of-day regexes (`magandang araw\|umaga\|...`, `naimbag nga aldaw\|bigat\|...`); or (b) longest greeting prefix (iterated over a length-sorted TUPLE, never the set — set order depended on PYTHONHASHSEED and flipped answers across restarts) whose remainder is an identity question, an addressee, or — English greetings only — a single word. `_conversation_response` then returns `IDENTITY_MESSAGE` if `_is_identity_question`, else a varied `CONVERSATION_MESSAGES` entry (default: "Hello! I'm IskAI. What CCSICT thesis topic would you like to explore?"). notice_type='conversation'. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2175-2187, 688-735, 862-871, 344-370` |
| 15 | service | FAST PATH 5 — self-directed provenance. `_is_system_origin_question` fullmatches `_SELF_ORIGIN_QUESTION` ('who developed/made/created/built/designed/programmed/coded/wrote you\|iskai\|this ai/assistant/chatbot/bot') or its Filipino/Ilocano twin `_FIL_SELF_ORIGIN_QUESTION`. Returns `SYSTEM_ORIGIN_MESSAGE` with notice_type='conversation'. No retrieval. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2193-2205, 810-816, 124-171` |
| 16 | service | REFUSAL. `prohibited_reason(req.question)` collapses whitespace and tests two things in order. (a) `_INJECTION.search` -> 'prompt_injection' (ignore/disregard/override previous instructions, reveal the system prompt, bypass the rules, act as a, pretend to be, developer mode, jailbreak, plus Tagalog/Ilocano forms: 'huwag mong sundin ang... utos', 'kalimutan mo ang... instruksyon', 'magpanggap ka', 'hindi ka na si iskai'). (b) `_is_generation_request` -> 'academic_content_generation'. On a hit: activity `chat_query_blocked` with `reason`, and `ChatResponse(answer=REFUSAL_MESSAGE, sources=[], session_id=...)` — note NO notice_type. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2207-2213; rag-thesis-backend/services/guards.py:514-521, 236-261` |
| 17 | service | WHY RETRIEVAL VERBS ARE ALLOWED. `_is_generation_request` needs a generation verb to GOVERN a prohibited artifact, not merely co-occur. `_EN_GENERATION_VERB` is a closed set — write\|draft\|compose\|generate\|create\|produce\|complete\|make\|rewrite\|expand\|extend\|formulate — so summarize, compare, list, explain, show, find, cite, count are structurally outside it. `_VERB_GOVERNS_ARTIFACT` requires verb + at most 8 tokens from a CLOSED `_MODIFIER` list + artifact; a content noun in between breaks the match. It must additionally be `_DIRECTED_AT_ASSISTANT` or an `_OWNED_ARTIFACT` ('my/our/aking/tesis ko'). `_GENERATE_OWNED_ARTIFACT` is the only clause that fires alone. Held out of `_ARTIFACT` on purpose: objectives, abstract, findings, results, talaan, listahan, pananaliksik, pag-aaral, layunin — the assistant's own working vocabulary. Hence 'summarize my thesis', 'help me improve my abstract', 'show me the lit review of that thesis', 'gumawa ka ng talaan ng mga tesis na gumamit ng CNN' are all in MUST_ALLOW. | `rag-thesis-backend/services/guards.py:492-511, 12-15, 106-129, 143-166, 205-229` |
| 18 | service | GUEST BUDGET (pre-retrieval). Guests only. `guest_budget.is_exhausted()` reads the UTC-day counter in the shared rate-limit store; returns False immediately when `GUEST_DAILY_TOKEN_BUDGET` is 0 (the dev/test/evaluation default). On exhaustion: activity `chat_query_budget_exhausted` stage=`pre_retrieval`, and `_guest_budget_response` -> `ChatResponse(answer=GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE, sources=[])`. A lookup failure fails OPEN (logged warning, returns 0). | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2218-2223, 1364-1365; rag-thesis-backend/services/guest_budget.py:51-54, 109-127` |
| 19 | db | HISTORY LOAD, with the notice filter that is the whole point of B14. `_load_chat_history` reads `chat_messages` with `.eq('kind', chat_notices.KIND_ANSWER)` applied BEFORE `.limit(5)` — so five usable exchanges come back even when recent history is mostly notices. `_chat_impl_unstamped` then re-filters in Python with `_is_stored_non_answer` (60-char prefix match against `NOTICE_MARKERS`) as defence in depth for pre-migration rows. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:1498-1508, 2236-2240; rag-thesis-backend/services/chat_notices.py:233-243, 179-192` |
| 20 | service | ARCHIVE INVENTORY (not a notice, but a non-RAG outcome that can be empty). On an inventory/continuation/count question, `list_archive_papers` is read directly. An empty archive returns 'The {department} archive currently has no indexed theses.' with `no_relevant_thesis=not inventory_total` -> True, `archive_current=True`. A lookup failure raises 503 `The thesis archive is temporarily unavailable. Please try again in a moment.` | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2286-2323, 1061-1062` |
| 21 | service | THREE CONTEXT-GATED SELF-REFERENCE PATHS. (a) `not reference_sources and _is_ambiguous_system_identity_question` ('ano ba itong system na ito', fullmatch only) -> `IDENTITY_MESSAGE`, notice_type='conversation'. (b) `_is_self_platform_reference` ('itong system na ginagamit ko') is UNGATED on purpose — it is a correction of a wrong retrieval answer whose own sources would otherwise block it — returning `SYSTEM_ORIGIN_MESSAGE` if a recent turn asked about origin, else `IDENTITY_MESSAGE`, notice_type='conversation'. (c) `not reference_sources and _is_ambiguous_system_origin_question` ('who developed this system') -> `SYSTEM_ORIGIN_MESSAGE` **with no notice_type**. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2329-2341, 2346-2361, 2369-2380` |
| 22 | service | THE 60s CAPACITY COOLDOWN GATE. `_capacity_limit_is_active()` is `time.monotonic() < _CAPACITY_STATE['limited_until']`. While it holds, `_capacity_response(req.session_id)` is returned: `ChatResponse(answer=CAPACITY_MESSAGE, sources=[], session_id=...)` — HTTP 200, no notice_type, no activity log, no provider call. Window is `settings.gemini_capacity_cooldown_seconds`, default 60. Note the position: this gate sits AFTER the five fast paths, the guest budget, the history load, the inventory path and the author fast path. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2426-2428, 1360-1361; rag-thesis-backend/services/chat_notices.py:194, 214-221; rag-thesis-backend/config.py:50` |
| 23 | service | POST-REWRITE REFUSAL. When an ambiguous follow-up with no prior sources is rewritten by Gemini (`_rewrite_followup`, which never raises — it falls back to `fallback_standalone_question` on any error), the rewritten wording is re-tested: `prohibited_reason(effective_question)` -> activity `chat_query_blocked` with `after_rewrite: True`, and the same `REFUSAL_MESSAGE` response. The rewrite call itself is charged to the guest budget FIRST and is refusable (stage=`followup_rewrite`). | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2528-2553, 1794-1812` |
| 24 | service | QUERY TRANSLATION. A non-English question is translated for retrieval only. The extra Gemini call is budget-charged (stage=`query_translation`, refusable for guests). `_translate_for_retrieval` never raises — any failure, including a 429, returns the question as asked and does NOT trip the cooldown. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2576-2593, 1815-1829` |
| 25 | service | RETRIEVAL. `_retrieve_evidence` embeds the query and runs `search_chunks` (RPC `match_chunks`, fixed 0.30 threshold, provenance params, candidate pool -> hybrid rerank -> per-paper cap -> exactly 5 blocks) concurrently with `check_topic_duplication`. An empty pool returns `('', [], 0.0)` from `search_chunks:803-804`. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:1958-1986; rag-thesis-backend/services/retriever.py:764-808` |
| 26 | service | RETRIEVAL FAILURE FORK. `except Exception` -> `logger.exception('Retrieval failed')`. If `_is_capacity_error(e)` (markers `resource_exhausted`\|`quota exceeded`\|`rate limit`\|`too many requests`, OR a LABELLED HTTP 429 via `mentions_http_status` — never a bare '429' substring), then `_mark_capacity_limited()` sets the 60s window and `_capacity_response` is returned with HTTP 200. Otherwise HTTP 503 `The thesis archive is temporarily unavailable. Please try again in a moment.` | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2628-2636; rag-thesis-backend/services/chat_notices.py:196-211; rag-thesis-backend/services/network_retry.py:34-56` |
| 27 | service | NO-RESULTS FALLBACK. `if not context:` returns `get_no_relevant_message(department)` = `'No relevant thesis was found in the {dept} archive for that query. Try rephrasing with different technical terms, or ask about another topic.'` (dept defaults to 'Isabela State University'), sources=[], `no_relevant_thesis=True`, `archive_current=True`, plus the duplication alert if one was raised (the alert used to be silently dropped here). **No activity row is logged on this path.** | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2644-2657, 1413-1421` |
| 28 | service | UNCLEAR-TOPIC NOTICE. `_is_unsupported_single_token_query`: exactly one alphanumeric token, length >= 4, and that token absent from the compacted concatenation of the context plus each source's title/authors/track/section_title. Returns `UNCLEAR_TOPIC_MESSAGE` ("I couldn't identify a thesis topic or research question in that message. Try a topic such as "artificial intelligence," or ask me to search by title, author, methodology, or finding.") with sources=[] — the retrieved sources are discarded. activity fast_path=`unclear_topic`. Costs one embedding + one RPC, no generation. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2659-2671, 738-762` |
| 29 | service | GUEST BUDGET (pre-generation). `estimate_charge` = tokens of (context + question + resolved question + history), measured with the chunker's `cl100k_base` proxy, plus `gemini_max_output_tokens`, booked atomically before the call. A refused charge still increments (documented race avoidance). -> `GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE`, stage=`pre_generation`. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2673-2691; rag-thesis-backend/services/guest_budget.py:99-107, 130-135` |
| 30 | external | GENERATION via `gemini_pool.arun(llm, CHAT, ...)`. `attempt_chain` = gateway (only if `LLM_BASE_URL` set and not cooling) -> primary Gemini key -> reserve keys (ready first, cooling demoted, never dropped). `_should_continue`: ANY gateway failure falls through to Google; on a Google hop only `is_capacity_error` rotates. Each failing hop with an api_key gets a `gemini_key_cooldown_seconds` (default 60) cooldown. When every hop fails, the LAST error is re-raised. | `rag-thesis-backend/services/gemini_pool.py:208-267, 235-252` |
| 31 | service | GENERATION FAILURE FORK — the mirror of step 26. `_is_capacity_error` -> `_mark_capacity_limited()` + `_capacity_response` at HTTP 200; anything else -> HTTP 502 `The research AI service is temporarily unavailable. Please try again later.` | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2732-2740` |
| 32 | service | SENTINEL + TRUNCATION READ, computed once and never recomputed. `prompts.strip_no_evidence_sentinel` removes `ARCHIVE_COVERAGE_INSUFFICIENT` and reports `True` only when it led the reply on its own line (`\A\s*` + optional emphasis wrapper + line terminator). Any other occurrence is stripped but never flagged — `<retrieved_context>` is untrusted, so a manuscript containing the token would otherwise be an injection primitive. `generation_truncated = is_truncated(result)` reads the provider's own `finish_reason` (`max_tokens` from Gemini, `length` from an OpenAI-compatible gateway, casefolded); a missing or non-dict `response_metadata` reads as NOT truncated. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2742-2763; rag-thesis-backend/services/prompts.py:57-96; rag-thesis-backend/services/llm_output.py:14-42` |
| 33 | service | MISDIRECTED GREETING. `_looks_like_misdirected_greeting` — the answer starts with 'hello'/'hi '/'hey ' AND contains "i'm iskai"\|'i am iskai'\|'iskai here'. A research question must never degrade into the introduction, so the answer is replaced by `_grounded_retrieval_fallback`. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2791-2794, 1290-1294` |
| 34 | service | TRUNCATION -> DISCARD, NOT REPAIR. `if generation_truncated:` logs the active output ceiling (`gemini_pool.active_output_ceiling()` — the gateway's 6000 or Gemini's 2000, whichever route applied) and the gateway flag, then replaces the answer wholesale with `_grounded_retrieval_fallback(sources, department)`: `'I could not verify a direct answer from the retrieved thesis text. The closest archived studies are "Title" — page N [1]; ... . Try asking about one of these titles.'` (at most 3 distinct papers). The repair ladder is skipped entirely — against a fragment it would restore markers and serve it as finished work. `unique_sources = filter_cited_sources(answer, sources)`, so the source cards survive and `no_relevant_thesis` stays False. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2796-2812, 1315-1352` |
| 35 | service | MODEL-REPORTED NO EVIDENCE. `elif reports_no_evidence:` (sentinel fired OR `_answer_reports_no_evidence` matched any of ~28 substrings including 'could not verify', 'does not mention', 'not enough information'). The model's own text and cited sources are KEPT — the repair ladder is bypassed deliberately, because `enforce_citation_coverage` maps uncited units to source 1 and would staple `[1]` onto a negative claim about the archive. Only if nothing survives (`not answer.strip() or not unique_sources`) does it degrade to `get_no_relevant_message(department)` with sources=[] and `no_relevant_thesis=True`. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2813-2833, 1297-1312` |
| 36 | service | CITATION LADDER (the normal branch). `validate_citations` + `_missing_referenced_papers`. On failure: one AI `_repair_citations` call (which itself raises `TruncatedGeneration` if the repair is severed), then deterministic `enforce_citation_coverage`. If both still fail, or the repair raises at all, the answer becomes `_grounded_retrieval_fallback` — the same honest degradation as step 34. A 429 here is caught by the bare `except Exception` and does NOT trip the cooldown. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2834-2888, 1832-1849` |
| 37 | service | `response.kind = chat_notices.response_kind(response)`. Order: `no_relevant_thesis` True -> notice; `notice_type == 'conversation'` -> notice; exact equality against CAPACITY_MESSAGE / REFUSAL_MESSAGE / GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE / all 10 CONVERSATION / IDENTITY / SYSTEM_ORIGIN / CAPABILITIES / all 10 COURTESY / all 10 FAREWELL / UNCLEAR_TOPIC -> notice; `startswith(NO_RELEVANT_PREFIX \| GROUNDED_FALLBACK_PREFIX)` -> notice; else answer. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2094; rag-thesis-backend/services/chat_notices.py:246-269` |
| 38 | db | PERSISTENCE (authenticated only). If `_client_gone(request)` is False, `_persist_chat_exchange` calls RPC `save_chat_exchange` with `p_kind = response_kind(response)`. The SQL raises `Unsupported chat message kind: %` for anything but 'answer'/'notice', verifies session ownership AND department, and inserts into `chat_messages(..., kind)`. The 8-argument signature is explicitly dropped so no caller can write an unmarked notice. A persistence failure is swallowed into `history_saved = False` (never fails the turn). | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2042-2065, 1601-1617; rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:626-684` |
| 39 | frontend | LIVE RENDER. The response is spread into the transcript with `kind: 'ai'` (the local role) and `messageKind: res.kind` — the rename is load-bearing, without it the API's classification would overwrite the role. `AiBubble` calls `messageNoticeLabel`: `no_relevant_thesis` -> amber chip 'Search completed · no qualifying archive evidence.'; `notice_type === 'conversation'` -> NO chip; `messageKind === 'notice'` -> amber chip 'System message · not a research answer.'; else no chip. `archive_current` separately renders a green 'Searched the current indexed archive' line. | `rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Chat.jsx:1161-1164, 726-752; rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/chat/messageNotice.js:17-35` |
| 40 | frontend | TRANSPORT FAILURE RENDER (the 4xx/5xx paths, i.e. steps 4-8, 20, 26-non-capacity, 31-non-capacity). `catch` in `send`: aborted requests return silently; otherwise the optimistic user bubble is removed (`m.slice(0, -1)`), the question is restored to the composer, `guestGate.handleChatError` reopens the Turnstile widget if the reply carried `x-guest-verification`, and a `role="alert"` banner shows 'IskAI could not answer' + `apiErrorMessage(err)` (FastAPI's `detail`) + the quoted question, plus a toast. | `rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Chat.jsx:1174-1182, 1351-1375; rag-thesis-frontend/src/lib/apiErrors.js:10-22` |
| 41 | frontend | PRIVILEGED-MFA REFUSAL — a sibling path, never reachable from /chat. `require_admin` / `require_superadmin` / `require_faculty_or_admin` / `require_novelty_access` / `require_upload_access` call `_require_privileged_mfa`, which raises 403 with the exact literal `'Multi-factor authentication is required for privileged access.'` when `REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA` is on and `_token_aal != 'aal2'`. The axios response interceptor matches it globally (`isPrivilegedMfaRefusal`: status 403 AND lowercase detail contains 'multi-factor authentication is required') and fires `reportPrivilegedMfaRequired()`; `PrivilegedMfaGate` then opens a modal offering the authenticator challenge or enrolment in place, rather than a Retry button or a trip back to /login (which would mint another aal1 session — a loop). | `rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py:224-240, 282-293, 419-451; rag-thesis-frontend/src/api.js:154; rag-thesis-frontend/src/lib/privilegedMfa.js:16-23, 66-71; rag-thesis-frontend/src/components/PrivilegedMfaGate.jsx:27-48` |
| 42 | api | RELOAD PATH. `GET /sessions/{id}/messages` selects `id,session_id,question,answer,sources,duplication_alert,kind,created_at` — notices are NOT filtered out, the conversation stays visible — and then recomputes `notice_type` in Python from the stored answer text (`chat_notices.notice_type`: legacy greeting prefix, or exact membership in CONVERSATION/IDENTITY/SYSTEM_ORIGIN/CAPABILITIES/COURTESY/FAREWELL). No `notice_type` column exists. `loadSession` maps `messageKind: m.kind, notice_type: m.notice_type` so the restored transcript reaches the same `messageNoticeLabel`. | `rag-thesis-backend/routers/sessions.py:22-25, 84-100; rag-thesis-backend/services/chat_notices.py:272-281; rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Chat.jsx:1052-1067` |
| 43 | service | EVALUATION CONSUMER. `run_comparison` calls `_chat_impl` directly (bypassing the route, so Turnstile and the persistence wrapper never run) and classifies only `CAPACITY_MESSAGE` and `GUEST_BUDGET_MESSAGE` as `_UNATTEMPTED_NOTICES` — retried up to `_UNATTEMPTED_MAX_ATTEMPTS` with `chat_notices.reset_capacity_limit()` first, so a retry is a real retry and not an instant replay of the cooldown. The no-relevant message, the grounded fallback and the guard refusal are deliberately EXCLUDED from that list: they are correct system outputs and are scored. | `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:82-99, 348-390` |
| 44 | service | LOAD-TEST CONSUMER. `summarize_chat_load.py` bands a JTL purely by latency because /chat's 200 does not mean success: `rc != 200` -> failure; `200 && <100 ms` -> capacity notice; `200 && 100-2000 ms` -> ambiguous; `200 && >=2000 ms` -> answer. Percentiles are reported over the answer band only. | `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/summarize_chat_load.py:21-31, 43-44, 66-100` |

### Branches

- Guest vs authenticated: guests get `THESIS_EVALUATION_DEPARTMENT`, an in-body `guest_history`/`guest_source_ids` context, a Turnstile challenge, a token budget, and NO persistence. Authenticated callers get a server-owned department, SQL-filtered history, and `save_chat_exchange`. (chat.py:2112-2123, 2257-2271; auth.py:107-108)
- Turnstile enabled vs not: the guard is entirely inert when `TURNSTILE_SECRET_KEY` is empty — the default, which keeps dev, the test suite and the frozen evaluation pipeline unchanged. (turnstile.py:36-38, 117-118)
- Guest budget enabled vs not: `GUEST_DAILY_TOKEN_BUDGET == 0` means unlimited and every check short-circuits to False/ALLOWED_UNLIMITED. `validate_production_services` makes a non-zero value mandatory under APP_ENVIRONMENT=production. (guest_budget.py:95-96, 123-127; config.py:112, 252)
- Capacity cooldown active vs not: active -> HTTP 200 + CAPACITY_MESSAGE in ~1-18 ms with zero provider calls; inactive -> the full pipeline runs and a 429 both trips the cooldown and returns the same notice. (chat.py:2426-2428, 2630-2632, 2734-2736)
- Gateway configured vs not: with `LLM_BASE_URL` set, the gateway is hop 1 and ANY failure falls through to Google, and the output ceiling reported on truncation is `llm_gateway_max_output_tokens` (6000) rather than `gemini_max_output_tokens` (2000). Embeddings are never routed. (gemini_pool.py:69-85, 228-232, 246-252, 126-130)
- Reserve keys configured vs not: with a single key `reserve_attempts` is empty and the control flow is byte-identical to having no pool, which is what keeps the evaluated pipeline frozen. (gemini_pool.py:18-22, 195-205)
- Truncated vs complete generation: truncated -> grounded fallback, ladder skipped, sources kept, `no_relevant_thesis=False`. Complete -> validation + up to two repair passes. (chat.py:2796-2812, 2834-2888)
- Sentinel/phrase no-evidence vs silent failure: `reports_no_evidence` keeps the model's own text and citations and bypasses validation; only an empty answer or zero cited sources degrades to the generic no-relevant notice with the flag set. (chat.py:2813-2833)
- Retrieval returned nothing vs something: empty context -> `no_relevant_thesis=True` + `archive_current=True`; non-empty but a single opaque token -> `UNCLEAR_TOPIC_MESSAGE` with sources dropped. (chat.py:2645-2671)
- `reference_sources` present vs absent: gates two of the three self-reference fast paths. 'who developed this system' / 'ano ba itong system na ito' answer as IskAI ONLY when the conversation holds no archived source for 'this' to point at; otherwise they stay retrieval questions. `_is_self_platform_reference` is deliberately ungated. (chat.py:2329, 2346, 2369)
- English vs Filipino/Ilocano: `_short_query_candidates` strips politeness particles and trailing addressees so each set holds one canonical entry; the single-trailing-word greeting shortcut is restricted to ENGLISH greetings because local speakers routinely open with greeting-then-topic. (chat.py:638-667, 712-718)
- Prompt edit (`edit_from_turn`) vs normal turn: history is loaded via `_history_before_turn` against the session's full ordered transcript, and truncation happens only AFTER the new answer completes, preserving the old branch on failure. (chat.py:1496-1497, 2044-2051)
- Client disconnected vs connected: `_client_gone` True suppresses persistence entirely, so a Stopped or edited-mid-flight turn does not reappear on reload. Ambiguous cases count as connected. (chat.py:1556-1581, 2042)

### Failure modes

- Invalid/expired JWT -> HTTP 401 `Invalid or expired authentication token` (WWW-Authenticate: Bearer). Frontend: axios interceptor tries one silent `refreshSession` and replays; if that fails it signs out and hard-navigates to `loginPathWithNext(current)`. (auth.py:256-260; api.js:127-147)
- Unapproved profile -> HTTP 403 `This account is pending approval or has been rejected.` Banner + Retry button (which will fail identically).
- Profiles table unreachable -> HTTP 503 `Account validation is temporarily unavailable.` (auth.py:147-150)
- Role lacks `chat` -> HTTP 403 `Research chat is not enabled for your role.` (auth.py:407-408)
- Missing Turnstile token -> HTTP 403 `Please complete the security check to continue as a guest.`, header `X-Guest-Verification: required`. Frontend `handleChatError` clears the session-storage pass flag, re-arms and remounts the widget with a bumped `resetKey`. (turnstile.py:124-128; useGuestChatGate.js:66-74)
- Cloudflare rejects the token, or verification is unreachable (fail closed) -> HTTP 403 `Security check failed or expired. Please try the check again.`, header `X-Guest-Verification: failed`. (turnstile.py:131-136, 103-108)
- Cross-department session reuse -> HTTP 409 `This conversation belongs to a different department. Start a new conversation.` (chat.py:1523-1527)
- Session not owned / mistyped uuid -> HTTP 404 `Session not found` (the uuid cast error is converted by `identifier_not_found` rather than surfacing as a 500). (chat.py:1491-1495, 1511-1522)
- Guest sends `session_id` -> HTTP 401 `Guest conversations do not have saved sessions.` (chat.py:2116-2117)
- Prompt injection or a request to author thesis content -> HTTP 200, `kind='notice'`, sources=[], answer = REFUSAL_MESSAGE: 'I can help you discover, compare, summarize, and cite existing archived studies, but I cannot write thesis chapters, assignments, proposals, hypotheses, or original academic arguments for you. Ask me what the archive contains about your topic instead.' Activity `chat_query_blocked` with reason `prompt_injection` or `academic_content_generation`. UI: amber chip 'System message · not a research answer.' Pinned by tests/test_guards_multilingual.py:353-364 (a MUST_REFUSE corpus and a larger MUST_ALLOW corpus asserted disjoint) and tests/test_chat_endpoint_flows.py:77-81.
- Guest daily allowance exhausted -> HTTP 200, `kind='notice'`, answer = "IskAI's shared daily allowance for guest research questions has been used up. Please sign in with your ISU account to continue, or try again tomorrow." Three distinct stages log it: `pre_retrieval`, `followup_rewrite`, `query_translation`, `pre_generation`. (guest_budget.py:51-54; chat.py:2218-2223, 2534-2542, 2579-2585, 2681-2691)
- Provider capacity, pre-emptive (cooldown holding) -> HTTP 200, `kind='notice'`, sources=[], answer = 'IskAI has reached the research AI service usage limit, so your question could not be processed right now. Please try again later.' No provider call, no activity row. Pinned by tests/test_chat_endpoint_flows.py:138-145 and tests/test_hardening_fixes.py:439-441.
- Provider capacity, discovered during retrieval or generation -> `_mark_capacity_limited()` sets the 60s window, then the identical HTTP 200 capacity notice. Pinned by tests/test_router_error_paths.py:250-255 and :124-128. The 429 recognition itself is pinned by tests/test_chat_notices.py:353-379 (six provider shapes recognised; five ordinary strings containing 429 digits rejected).
- Retrieval fails for any non-capacity reason -> HTTP 503 `The thesis archive is temporarily unavailable. Please try again in a moment.` Pinned by tests/test_router_error_paths.py:257-259.
- Generation fails for any non-capacity reason -> HTTP 502 `The research AI service is temporarily unavailable. Please try again later.` (chat.py:2737-2740)
- Archive inventory / author lookup / exact-title lookup fails -> HTTP 503, same archive message. The title-FRAGMENT lookup is the exception: it is a guess, so its failure is logged and degrades to ordinary follow-up handling. (chat.py:2297-2302, 2396-2401, 2441-2446 vs 2463-2472)
- Nothing above the 0.30 threshold -> HTTP 200, `kind='notice'`, `no_relevant_thesis=True`, `archive_current=True`, sources=[], answer = 'No relevant thesis was found in the {department} archive for that query. Try rephrasing with different technical terms, or ask about another topic.' UI: amber chip 'Search completed · no qualifying archive evidence.' plus the green 'Searched the current indexed archive' line. Pinned by tests/test_chat_endpoint_flows.py:1111-1119 and tests/test_chat_notices.py:93-97.
- Empty archive for an inventory question -> HTTP 200, `no_relevant_thesis=True`, answer 'The {department} archive currently has no indexed theses.' (chat.py:1061-1062, 2321)
- Opaque single-token query that embeddings matched by accident -> HTTP 200, `kind='notice'`, UNCLEAR_TOPIC_MESSAGE, retrieved sources discarded. (chat.py:2659-2671)
- Generation severed at the output ceiling (`finish_reason` max_tokens/length) -> HTTP 200, `kind='notice'` (via GROUNDED_FALLBACK_PREFIX), `no_relevant_thesis=False`, sources KEPT, answer = 'I could not verify a direct answer from the retrieved thesis text. The closest archived studies are ... Try asking about one of these titles.' Entirely pinned by tests/test_generation_truncation.py — :90-108 (fallback served, fragment text absent, flag False, both sources kept), :110-125 (the repair ladder never sees the fragment), :127-143 (a complete reply is untouched), :147-154 and :191-201 (both repairs raise `TruncatedGeneration` rather than returning a fragment), :166-184 (end-to-end).
- Citation repair exhausted, or the repair call itself raised -> same grounded fallback. (chat.py:2874-2887)
- Model emitted only a no-evidence statement with no surviving cited sources -> generic no-relevant message, sources=[], `no_relevant_thesis=True`. (chat.py:2828-2833)
- Persistence failed -> the answer still returns, with `history_saved: false`; the UI raises a warning toast 'Answer received, but chat history was not saved'. (chat.py:2063-2065; Chat.jsx:1165-1169)
- User pressed Stop -> the request is aborted client-side, the optimistic bubble is dropped by `dropPendingPrompt`, a 'Response stopped' alert preserves the question, and `_client_gone` suppresses server-side persistence. (Chat.jsx:1198-1213; transcript.js:24-27; chat.py:1556-1581)
- Rate limit exceeded (app-side, not provider) -> slowapi's own 429 body, NOT a ChatResponse and NOT the capacity notice. It reaches the frontend as a generic error banner. (chat.py:2008-2009; main.py:154)
- Privileged-MFA refusal -> HTTP 403 `Multi-factor authentication is required for privileged access.` on /upload, /duplication and admin routes; the string is pinned verbatim by tests/test_auth_authorization.py:200-223 across five guards, and :225-235 pins the inverse (a plain role refusal must NOT use that wording). Frontend: `PrivilegedMfaGate` modal offering the authenticator challenge or enrolment.

### What gets persisted

- `chat_messages` row per authenticated exchange, written through RPC `save_chat_exchange` with `p_kind` = 'answer'|'notice' (supabase_setup.sql:626-670). Notices ARE persisted and ARE shown in the transcript; they are excluded only from the model-context loader. The function raises on any other kind value and the 8-arg signature was dropped so no caller can write an unmarked notice (supabase_setup.sql:645-647, 682-684).
- `chat_sessions` row created on the first exchange of a new conversation, titled from the first 40 chars of the question (chat.py:1606; supabase_setup.sql:649-652). A capacity notice or a refusal as the first turn therefore CREATES a session titled after the refused question.
- `activity` rows via `log_activity` in a BackgroundTask: `chat_query` with a `fast_path` label for model_identity / capabilities / thanks / farewell / conversation / system_origin / archive_inventory / system_identity / self_platform / author_metadata / query_translated / unclear_topic, plus the terminal grounded-answer row; `chat_query_blocked` with `reason` and optional `after_rewrite`; `chat_query_budget_exhausted` with `stage` and `charged`. (chat.py:2127, 2143, 2162, 2176, 2194, 2209, 2219, 2303, 2330, 2347, 2370, 2408, 2536, 2548, 2580, 2588, 2660, 2686, 2890)
- Guest token counter: an atomic increment against a `guest-token-budget:YYYY-MM-DD` key in the shared rate-limit storage (Redis in production), expiring just after the UTC day. A REFUSED request still increments. (guest_budget.py:49, 79-92, 130-135)
- Process-local, non-durable state: `_CAPACITY_STATE['limited_until']` (chat_notices.py:194), `gemini_pool._cooldowns` and `_clients` (gemini_pool.py:62-65), `turnstile._verified_guests` (turnstile.py:33 — explicitly in-process and documented as needing Redis for multi-replica). None of these survive a restart or are shared between replicas.
- Browser: `sessionStorage['iskai_guest_gate_ok']` marks a passed Turnstile check (useGuestChatGate.js:4, 58).
- Nothing about a capacity outage, a refusal count, or a truncation event is written to the database; they exist only in the log line and, for refusals and budget exhaustion, in the `activity` table.

### Surprises

- **POST /chat can never return the privileged-MFA refusal.** `require_chat_access` deliberately does not call `_require_privileged_mfa`, with a comment saying reading the archive and asking a research question are ordinary user features and requiring aal2 would lock an administrator out of the library itself (auth.py:384-388). The refusal only ever reaches the chat screen as a side effect of some other endpoint, through the global axios interceptor at api.js:154.
- **The '60s process-wide cooldown' is chat-only.** `_mark_capacity_limited()` has exactly two call sites, both in routers/chat.py (2631, 2735), and `_capacity_limit_is_active()` exactly one (2427). Duplication scanning, upload metadata extraction and embedding all go through `gemini_pool` and neither trip nor read it. A 429 that exhausts the pool during a novelty scan leaves chat entirely unaware.
- **Four Gemini calls per turn cannot produce the capacity notice at all.** `_rewrite_followup` (1810-1812), `_translate_for_retrieval` (1827-1829), `_summarize_duplication` (1905-1907) and both repair calls (2788-2789, 2881-2887) swallow every exception including a 429. A capacity failure there degrades to a fallback question, the untranslated query, an empty duplication summary, or the grounded fallback — never the capacity notice, and never a cooldown.
- **`reports_no_evidence` disables citation validation for the whole answer.** Any grounded answer containing one of ~28 substrings anywhere — 'does not mention', 'do not provide', 'could not find', 'cannot answer' — takes the `elif` at chat.py:2813, skipping `validate_citations` and both repair passes entirely. It is served with whatever markers it happens to carry, stamped `kind='answer'`, and no downstream check looks at it. The bypass is deliberate and documented (stapling `[1]` onto a negative claim about the archive is a faithfulness defect), but its blast radius is any answer that mentions a gap in passing.
- **Two identical SYSTEM_ORIGIN_MESSAGE responses render differently.** The self-origin path sets `notice_type='conversation'` (chat.py:2204); the ambiguous-origin path, 170 lines later, does not (chat.py:2376-2380). Same text, same `kind='notice'`, but the first shows no chip and the second shows the amber 'System message · not a research answer.' No test covers the difference — tests/test_chat_endpoint_flows.py:1044 asserts only the answer string.
- **The model-identity reply gains a warning chip after a page reload.** Live it carries `notice_type='conversation'` (chat.py:2137) so `messageNoticeLabel` returns null. `response_kind` checks `notice_type` before anything else (chat_notices.py:256), so the row is persisted with `kind='notice'`.
