### Branches

- Concurrency 1 (default): no ThreadPoolExecutor at all; `process_claimed_job` runs INLINE on the loop thread (ingestion_worker.py:317-319). Consequence: `house.tick()` cannot run for the whole duration of a job, so cleanup, `expire_upload_jobs` and the operations monitor are blocked until the manuscript finishes. Only the LeaseHeartbeat background thread keeps the registry warm — and only until the commit.
- Concurrency > 1: pooled; when `len(in_flight) >= concurrency` the loop blocks in `wait(..., FIRST_COMPLETED)` instead of claiming. `_Housekeeping.tick(next(iter(in_flight.values()), None))` reports only ONE arbitrary in-flight job id to the registry, so `ingestion_workers.current_job_id` under-reports at concurrency > 1 (ingestion_worker.py:298,304-306).
- `--once`: forces concurrency 1 whatever the setting, and returns immediately (processed count) on an empty queue, a claim exception, or an unavailable scanner (ingestion_worker.py:281,300-301,311-312,324-325).
- Scanner unavailable: no claim this tick. With `--once` the worker exits having processed 0 and says nothing about why; in the long-running mode it sleeps a poll interval and `evaluate_operations` raises a critical `scanner_unavailable` alert (ingestion_worker.py:299-303, operations.py:318-320,342-345).
- OCR present vs absent: a scanned page with no OCR binding lands in `unresolved_scanned_pages` and, with `REQUIRE_OCR_FOR_SCANNED_PAGES=true`, permanently fails the job naming each page. Set false and the manuscript is indexed short those pages (ingestion.py:127-137).
- Duplication verdict: `exact_duplicate` (every chunk >= 0.999) refuses before commit; `high_overlap` (concentration >= 70) and `review_suggested` (>= 25) are committed with the scan attached and surfaced as a warning toast; `clear` (< 25) is committed silently (novelty.py:92-106,114-123; ingestion.py:172-183).
- Duplication percentage bookkeeping: `matched_chunk_percentage`/`duplication_percentage` are coverage, but the VERDICT keys off `top_paper_percentage` (concentration). A paper can show 97% coverage and still be graded `clear` (novelty.py:57-106,159-180).
- Ambiguous commit response: the deterministic paper/job id pair makes recovery possible; only an exact status+chunk_count match is accepted as committed, otherwise the original error is re-raised (ingestion.py:249-274).
- `heartbeat_upload_job_control` missing (a project built from supabase_setup.sql WITHOUT the 20260724 migration): `heartbeat_job_control` detects PGRST202/42883 and silently degrades to `heartbeat_upload_job`, hardcoding `cancel_requested: False`. Cancellation during `processing` then never reaches the worker at all (upload_queue.py:62-73).
- `register_ingestion_worker` / `stop_ingestion_worker` missing: `operations._missing_rpc` swallows it and returns False, so the worker runs with no registry row — which makes `evaluate_operations` report `worker_unavailable` critical (operations.py:47-74, operations.py:322-326).
- Retry vs fail: `attempt_count < max_attempts AND is_retryable_ingestion_error(error)` -> `schedule_upload_retry`; anything else -> `fail_upload_job` (ingestion_worker.py:200-204).

### Failure modes

- Storage download returns a non-bytes payload -> `RuntimeError('Private storage returned an invalid payload')`. NOT a PermanentIngestionError, so it is evaluated by `is_retryable_ingestion_error`, which will usually say no (no status code, no transient marker) -> `fail_upload_job` with the public line 'The thesis could not be safely indexed after the allowed retries.' (ingestion.py:56-62, upload_queue.py:135-149).
- Staged bytes no longer hash to `content_sha256` -> `PermanentIngestionError('The staged PDF hash did not match the reserved upload')` -> no retry -> job `failed`, `error='The manuscript could not be processed safely.'`, `cleanup_status='pending'`. Frontend: red 'Ingestion failed' card with that sentence (ingestion.py:65-67, upload_queue.py:139-143, IngestOutcome.jsx:161-175).
- Encrypted PDF / unreadable PDF / page count outside 1..500 -> Permanent, same fixed public sentence. Note the API already rejected these at upload time with HTTP 422, so hitting them in the worker means the staged object changed (upload.py:497-511 vs ingestion.py:70-82).
- ClamAV reports FOUND -> `MalwareDetectedIngestionError` (Permanent). `process_claimed_job` additionally upserts a `malware:{job_id}` CRITICAL operational alert with `{'action':'rejected_and_cleanup_queued'}` and fires the signed webhook, then fails the job; alert persistence failures are logged, never raised (ingestion_worker.py:190-199).
- ClamAV unreachable / garbage response -> `MalwareScannerUnavailable` carrying `status_code = 503` -> RETRYABLE -> `schedule_upload_retry`, job goes to `retry_wait` with message 'A temporary service problem occurred. The job will retry automatically.' and `error=null`. The timeline shows the amber 'Temporary service interruption. Automatic retry N/M is scheduled for HH:MM:SS.' banner (malware.py:13-16, upload_queue.py:83-100, PipelineTimeline.jsx:159-164).
- No extractable text / no clean indexable chunks / invalid chunk records -> Permanent, fixed public sentence.
- Unresolved scanned pages with OCR required -> Permanent, and this is the ONE Permanent error whose long message is discarded: `fail_job` publishes the fixed sentence, so the uploader never sees 'Install the tesserocr runtime'; only the worker log has it (ingestion.py:127-137 vs upload_queue.py:136-143).
- Gemini embedding capacity/rate error -> `retry_transient` burns 3 attempts (0.5/1.0 s), then `gemini_pool.run` walks the reserve-key chain; if it still raises, the error usually carries 429/5xx and is retryable -> `schedule_upload_retry` (embedder.py:71-98, gemini_pool.py:270-282, upload_queue.py:91-99).
- Embedding count/dimension mismatch -> Permanent, fixed sentence.
- Exact duplicate -> `DuplicateManuscriptIngestionError`, Permanent, and the ONLY error whose own text reaches the user: 'This manuscript was not indexed because it is an exact duplicate of "<title>" (<year>), which is already in the archive: all N passages matched at X% similarity.' The frontend regexes `/exact duplicate/i` on `job.error` and renders 'Already in the archive' + 'View archive' (ingestion.py:34-53,176-183, IngestOutcome.jsx:160-172).
- Heartbeat RPC throws once: `update()` logs a warning, sets `_valid = False` PERMANENTLY, and returns False. The keep-alive thread exits; the pipeline thread keeps working until the next checkpoint, where `_require_lease` raises `LeaseLostError`. `process_claimed_job` catches it and records NOTHING — no retry, no failure. The job stays `processing` with a lease that expires up to 120 s later, after which `claim_upload_job` re-claims it (attempt_count+1). Net cost: one whole attempt plus the lease timeout, for one network blip (ingestion_worker.py:140-147,184-185, ingestion.py:85-87).
- Lease genuinely stolen (another worker re-claimed after expiry): `heartbeat_upload_job_control`'s `lease_owner = p_worker_id` predicate fails -> `lease_valid:false` -> `LeaseLostError` -> silent stop. `commit_upload_ingestion` independently raises 'Upload worker lease is no longer valid' if the loser reaches the commit first (operations_security.sql:240-245,432-433).
- Lease expired with retries left and nobody re-claimed: nothing fails the job; it simply sits `processing` until the next `claim_upload_job`, whose selection clause treats `processing AND lease_expires_at < now()` as claimable (operations_security.sql:388).
- Lease expired with `attempt_count >= max_attempts`: the sweep inside `claim_upload_job` sets `failed`, `failure_category='worker_lease_expired'`, `error='Please submit the manuscript again later.'`, `cleanup_status='pending'` when `source_stored` (operations_security.sql:375-384).
- `schedule_upload_retry` returns false (lease already gone): logged as 'Retry was not scheduled because job %s no longer owns its lease'; the job is left `processing` with a dead lease and is picked up by the expiry sweep on a later claim (ingestion_worker.py:201-202).
- `fail_upload_job` returns false (lease already gone): logged as 'Failure was not recorded because job %s no longer owns its lease'; same recovery path (ingestion_worker.py:203-204).
- Cancellation requested while `processing`: `request_upload_cancellation` sets `cancel_requested_at` and the message 'Cancellation requested. The worker will stop at the next safe checkpoint.'; HTTP 200 with `outcome='cancellation_requested'`. The next control heartbeat returns `cancel_requested:true`, the keep-alive thread raises internally and exits, and the pipeline's next `_require_lease` raises `CancellationRequested` WITHOUT sending an RPC. `finalize_upload_cancellation` then writes `status='cancelled', stage='cancelled', progress=100, message='Upload cancelled.'`, `cleanup_status='pending'` (operations_security.sql:183-191,250-273, ingestion_worker.py:124-147,179-183).
- Cancellation requested while `staging`/`queued`/`retry_wait`: the RPC transitions the row to `cancelled` immediately in one statement, `outcome='cancelled'`, message 'Upload cancelled and private-source cleanup queued.' (operations_security.sql:192-206, upload.py:1251-1255).
- Cancellation requested for a terminal job: `outcome='already_terminal'`, HTTP 200, message 'Upload is already {status}.' A non-owner non-superadmin gets `outcome='forbidden'` -> HTTP 403 'You cannot cancel this upload job'; unknown id -> HTTP 404 (upload.py:1245-1250).
- Cancellation lands in the window between the `index` checkpoint and the commit RPC: `commit_upload_ingestion` raises 'Upload cancellation was requested'. That is a plain PostgREST error, not `CancellationRequested`, so `process_claimed_job` falls into the generic `except Exception` branch, finds it non-retryable, and writes `status='failed'` with 'The thesis could not be safely indexed after the allowed retries.' The user cancelled and is shown a red failure card blaming retries (operations_security.sql:431, ingestion_worker.py:186-204).
- Cancellation finalization loses the lease: logged 'Cancellation could not finalize because job %s lost its lease'; the job is rescued by the second sweep in `claim_upload_job`, which cancels expired-lease `processing` rows whose `cancel_requested_at` is set (ingestion_worker.py:182-183, operations_security.sql:368-374).
- Post-commit failures cannot undo the paper: `log_activity` and `refresh_after_ingest` both swallow everything (`logger.exception` / `logger.warning`) by design (ingestion.py:276-301, novelty.py:485-487,514-515).
- Cleanup of a failed/cancelled job's staged PDF: `claim_upload_cleanup` leases the row for 120 s; storage remove failure is delegated to `storage_cleanup_queue` via `record_storage_cleanup`; if even that insert fails the function returns False WITHOUT calling `finish_upload_cleanup`, leaving `cleanup_status='processing'` — recovered by `expire_upload_jobs`, which resets `processing` cleanups with an expired lease back to `pending` (upload_queue.py:152-184, cleanup.py:8-34, operations_security.sql:449-472).
- Poll-side failures in the browser: HTTP 404 stops polling with 'This upload job has expired or is no longer available.'; five consecutive other failures stop with 'The server could not confirm the upload status. The job was not cancelled.' with linearly backed-off retries at 1.5 s × failure count (Upload.jsx:337-350).
- Status read with a malformed job id: `is_invalid_identifier` converts the Postgres uuid cast error into HTTP 404 'Upload job not found (it may have expired)' rather than a 503 that would invite retries (upload.py:1149-1157).

### What gets persisted

- `upload_jobs` (the claimed row, mutated in place): claim sets status/stage/progress/message/attempt_count/lease_owner/lease_expires_at/heartbeat_at/started_at; each checkpoint updates stage/progress/message/lease_expires_at/heartbeat_at; commit sets status='completed', stage='done', progress=100, paper_id, chunks, duplication (a copy of the whole scan), cleanup_status='not_required', completed_at, expires_at=+30 days.
- `upload_job_events` — one row per INSERT, per `cancel_requested_at` transition, per status change, per stage change, per cleanup_status change, written by the AFTER trigger `record_upload_job_event` with sanitized `details`. FK is `on delete set null` so events outlive the 30-day job row (migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:98-142,37-41).
- `papers` — exactly one row, id == job id, with title/authors/year/abstract/track/filename/storage_path/chunk_count/duplication_scan/uploaded_by/department/active_index_version/ingestion_status/redaction_stats. `content` is inserted from a key the worker never sends, so it is always NULL (documented at supabase_setup.sql:1188-1196).
- `papers` again, via the BEFORE INSERT hydrate trigger: program_id, specialization_id, legacy_track, classification_status, thesis_category, read back out of `upload_jobs.request_payload`.
- `paper_index_versions` — one row per index build: paper_id, index_version, embedding_model, embedding_dimensions, preprocessing_version, chunking_version, tokenizer, chunk_size_tokens, chunk_overlap_tokens, provenance_status='verified', activated_at.
- `chunks` — one row per surviving chunk: paper_id, chunk_index, content, embedding (vector(768)), metadata jsonb, page_start, page_end, section, index_version. FK (paper_id, index_version) -> paper_index_versions ON DELETE RESTRICT.
- `activity_log` — one `paper_upload` row with paper_id/title/track/chunks/duplication_flagged/matched_chunk_percentage/highest_similarity, written through `dependencies.auth.sb`.
- `papers.duplication_scan.rescan` — `refresh_after_ingest` rewrites this nested object for every OTHER paper in the department whose nearest-neighbour table moved, preserving the at-upload figures alongside it.
- `ingestion_workers` — upserted by every registry heartbeat (worker_id, state, scanner_status, version '2.1.0', current_job_id, last_seen_at) and set to state='stopping' on shutdown.
- `operational_alerts` — `malware:{job_id}` on a malware rejection, and (when the monitor is enabled) the deduped worker_unavailable / queue_depth / queue_age / cleanup_delayed / retries_exhausted / scanner_unavailable / backup_stale keys.
- `storage_cleanup_queue` — only when a storage `remove()` fails during cleanup or staging compensation.
- Supabase Storage bucket `pdfs`, path `uploads/{owner_id}/{job_id}/{safe_filename}` — staged by the API and, on success, NEVER deleted: it becomes `papers.storage_path`, the archive's permanent PDF. Only failed/cancelled jobs queue it for removal.

### Surprises

- NOTHING IS RESUMABLE. A retry re-runs download, hash, ClamAV, extract+OCR, chunk, embed, screen and commit from scratch — the only state carried across attempts is the staged PDF in the `pdfs` bucket and `request_payload`. No intermediate text, chunks or embeddings are ever persisted. `stage`/`progress` on the job row are display-only; nothing reads them to decide where to resume. The single idempotency point is the far end: `commit_upload_ingestion` short-circuits on an already-`completed` job and `commit_paper_ingestion` short-circuits on an already-`ready` paper with a matching chunk count (operations_security.sql:430, 20260720_index_embedding_provenance.sql:146-155).
- `activate_paper_index` IS NOT IN THIS FLOW. Despite being named alongside the commit RPCs, the worker never calls it — `commit_paper_ingestion` writes `active_index_version` directly in the papers INSERT (20260720_index_embedding_provenance.sql:160,168). The only caller anywhere in the codebase is `scripts/reindex_citations.py:235`, the offline rebuild path.
- The 600-second rung of the retry ladder is UNREACHABLE at the default settings. `_RETRY_DELAYS = (30, 120, 600)` is indexed by `attempt_count - 1`, but `schedule_retry` only runs while `attempt_count < max_attempts` and `ingestion_max_attempts` defaults to 3. Attempt 1 -> 30 s, attempt 2 -> 120 s, attempt 3 -> straight to `fail_upload_job`. Index 2 is dead unless someone sets max_attempts >= 4 (upload_queue.py:13,117-123, ingestion_worker.py:200).
- `LeaseHeartbeat.__enter__` returns `self.update`, a bound METHOD, not the object. `process_ingestion_job(client, job, worker_id, heartbeat)` therefore takes a callable, and `_require_lease(heartbeat, **updates)` is just calling it. Reading `with LeaseHeartbeat(...) as heartbeat` as 'heartbeat is the LeaseHeartbeat' is wrong (ingestion_worker.py:116-118,177-178).
- A SINGLE transient heartbeat exception permanently poisons the attempt. `update()`'s except block sets `self._valid = False` with no path back to True, so the next checkpoint raises `LeaseLostError`, which `process_claimed_job` catches and logs WITHOUT scheduling a retry or recording a failure. The job silently sits in `processing` until its lease expires and a later `claim_upload_job` picks it up, consuming another attempt (ingestion_worker.py:140-147,184-185).
- The worker's `client` argument is NOT the client the whole pipeline uses. `client` is only used for the storage download, the commit RPC and the recovery reads. `screen_new_submission`, `refresh_after_ingest` and `_archive_size` all go through the module-level `services.retriever.sb`, and `log_activity` goes through `dependencies.auth.sb` — three separate Supabase client objects in one job (ingestion.py:106,242; novelty.py:28; activity.py:5).
- Consequence for the test suite: `patch_pipeline` mocks `extract_document`, `split_document`, `embed_texts`, `screen_new_submission` and `log_activity`, but NOT `refresh_after_ingest`. Since it uses the unpatched module-level `sb` pointed at the fake `https://test-project.supabase.co`, `TestPipeline::test_verified_pipeline_uses_atomic_job_completion` makes a real outbound HTTPS attempt that is swallowed by the function's blanket `except Exception` (tests/test_durable_ingestion.py:146-161,169; tests/conftest.py:29; novelty.py:485-487).
- A cancellation that lands in the window between the `index` checkpoint and the commit RPC ends the job as `failed` with 'The thesis could not be safely indexed after the allowed retries.', not as `cancelled`. `commit_upload_ingestion` raises a plain SQL error for `cancel_requested_at`, which does not travel as `CancellationRequested` and so misses the cancellation branch in `process_claimed_job` entirely (operations_security.sql:431, ingestion_worker.py:179-204).
- A successful upload NEVER cleans up its staged file, by design: `commit_upload_ingestion` sets `cleanup_status='not_required'` but leaves `source_stored=true`, and the staged path is also `papers.storage_path`. The 'staged private copy' is the archive PDF. `expire_upload_jobs` deletes the job row 30 days later while the storage object and paper survive (operations_security.sql:438-444,473-477).
- `papers.content` is always NULL. `commit_paper_ingestion`'s INSERT reads `p_paper->>'content'`, a key the worker never puts in `paper_data`. This is deliberate and documented as an access-model delimitation, but the column and the INSERT term both still exist (supabase_setup.sql:513,519,1188-1196; ingestion.py:191-214).
- `upload_jobs.thesis_category` and `upload_jobs.classification_status` are dead columns. `reserve_upload_job`'s INSERT column list does not include either, so every job row sits at the defaults 'student' and 'unclassified' regardless of what was uploaded. The real values live only inside `request_payload` and are hydrated onto `papers` by the trigger. Both are labelled 'forward-compat mirror' in the DDL (supabase_setup.sql:1341-1343,1470-1479; 20260819_thesis_category.sql:19-22).
- `heartbeat_upload_job_control`, `finalize_upload_cancellation`, `request_upload_cancellation`, `register_ingestion_worker`, `stop_ingestion_worker` and the `upload_job_events`/`ingestion_workers`/`operational_alerts` tables exist ONLY in migrations/20260724, never in supabase_setup.sql — while the cancellation COLUMNS were back-ported into the base file (with a long comment explaining why). `tests/test_schema_consistency.py::test_no_undeclared_drift` iterates `set(base) & set(newest)`, so a function absent from the base file is invisible to the guard (supabase_setup.sql:1359-1373 vs tests/test_schema_consistency.py:136-152).
- `heartbeat_upload_job` (the non-control RPC) is effectively dead in a fully migrated project: the only caller is the PGRST202/42883 fallback inside `heartbeat_job_control`, which hardcodes `cancel_requested: False` and therefore silently disables cooperative cancellation on an under-migrated database (upload_queue.py:33-73).
- `validate_chunk_records` runs twice and the second run RENUMBERS. `split_document` returns already-validated records, then ingestion filters noise and validates again, which reassigns `chunk_index` contiguously over the survivors — so stored chunk indices are dense and do not correspond to pre-filter split order. The stale `start_index`/`end_index` fields survive in the dicts but are never persisted (chunker.py:184-212,303; ingestion.py:144-153).
- The 85% duplication threshold and the 0.999 exact-duplicate band are different things, and the verdict is computed from neither directly. 0.85 decides whether a chunk counts as matched at all; 0.999-on-every-chunk decides refusal; the clear/review/high_overlap label comes from CONCENTRATION on one archived thesis (25/70 bands), explicitly because coverage saturates toward 100% in a single-department archive (novelty.py:48,57-106).
- `document_processor` carries four unused public helpers — `extract_pdf_text`, `extract_text`, `filter_noise_chunks`, `_remove_excluded_sections` (superseded by `_remove_excluded_sections_from_pages`) — plus `chunker.split_text` and `novelty.meets_duplication_threshold`. None is referenced anywhere in routers/, services/, workers/, scripts/ or evaluation/.
- `is_retryable_ingestion_error`'s `LeaseLostError` exclusion (upload_queue.py:84) is unreachable from the worker: `process_claimed_job` catches `LeaseLostError` in its own branch before the generic handler that would consult it.
- `queue_upload_job` sets `next_retry_at = now()` on a brand-new queued job, and `claim_upload_job` orders by `coalesce(next_retry_at, created_at)` — so FIFO is by queue-completion time, not creation time, and a retried job (future next_retry_at) always sorts behind fresh work (supabase_setup.sql:1511, operations_security.sql:390).
- The `stage` and `progress` columns keep advancing after a cancellation is requested even though the `message` column is frozen, because `heartbeat_upload_job_control` only guards the message with the `cancel_requested_at is null` case expression (operations_security.sql:236-238). In practice the pipeline thread short-circuits before sending another RPC, so at most one further stage update slips through.

**Adversarial fact-check:** verdict `mostly-accurate`, 9 correction(s), 9 omission(s). Corrections are already folded into the text above. They are kept because each records a wrong belief a competent reader reached from the same files - the same trap is available to you.

- **Wrong:** Step 24 / latencyNotes anchor the embedder to `embedder.py:60`, `embedder.py:71-98`, `89-98`, `92-97`.
  **Actual:** `rag-thesis-backend/services/embedder.py` is 55 lines long; every one of those anchors is past EOF. The real lines are: `_BATCH_SIZE = 64` at rag-thesis-backend/services/embedder.py:17, `_with_retry` at rag-thesis-backend/services/embedder.py:28-35, `embed_texts` (the ceil(N/64) sequential loop) at rag-thesis-backend/services/embedder.py:46-55, and `embeddings_model` constructed at module import at rag-thesis-backend/services/embedder.py:21-25. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/embedder.py:17,28-35,46-55`)
- **Wrong:** Step 27 describes the live `match_chunks` and anchors it to `migrations/20260720_index_embedding_provenance.sql:224-255` plus `migrations/20260819_thesis_category.sql:74-82`.
  **Actual:** Both are superseded. Migrations apply in filename order, so the authoritative definition is `migrations/20260903_hnsw_ef_search.sql:35-69`. It differs in two ways the trace never mentions: it carries a seventh parameter `p_thesis_category text default null` with predicate `and (p_thesis_category is null or p.thesis_category = p_thesis_category)` (line 66), and it is declared `set hnsw.ef_search = 100` (line 50) precisely because the six equality predicates are applied after the HNSW scan produces candidates. The six predicates the trace lists are otherwise character-identical (lines 59-65). Note also that `screen_new_submission` (novelty.py:353-359) omits `p_thesis_category` entirely, so the ingest-time screen compares a student thesis against faculty theses too. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260903_hnsw_ef_search.sql:35-69`)
- **Wrong:** The flow summary calls it "the eight-stage pipeline".
  **Actual:** There are seven worker stages and seven `_require_lease` checkpoints: download(12), malware_scan(16), extract(20), chunk(40), embed(58), screen(72), index(88). The trace's own step list stops at "Checkpoint 7 (last)". The frontend agrees: `PIPELINE_STAGES` is documented as "The seven durable-worker stages, in the order the worker reports them" and holds exactly seven entries. (`rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/upload/wizardSteps.js:17-26`)
- **Wrong:** Trigger: `queue_upload_job` "happens at the end of POST /upload/paper (rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:842)".
  **Actual:** Line 842 is correct but it is not inside `upload_paper`. It sits in the shared helper `_stage_and_queue_one` (routers/upload.py:764-879), whose docstring says "Shared by the single-file and batch endpoints so both keep the same two-phase commit and compensation." `upload_paper` is defined at routers/upload.py:885 and `upload_batch` at routers/upload.py:1001, reaching the same helper through `_stage_batch_file` (routers/upload.py:1018). So the worker's queue is also fed by POST /upload/batch, which the trace never mentions. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:764-769,842,1018`)
- **Wrong:** Step 6: "an expired-lease job is re-claimable in the same statement that would have failed it — the ordering matters."
  **Actual:** The sweeps and the selection are separate statements inside one function, and the pair in question is disjoint, so the ordering does not matter for it. Sweep (c) fires only on `attempt_count >= max_attempts` (line 384); the selection requires `attempt_count < max_attempts` (line 389). No job is ever both failed by the sweep and re-claimed by the select. What is true is the narrower claim already in the failureModes list: an expired-lease job with attempts left is untouched by all three sweeps and falls straight through to the selection via `status = 'processing' and lease_expires_at < now()` (line 388). (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:375-391`)
- **Wrong:** Step 14 / failureModes: "A non-bytes payload raises `RuntimeError('Private storage returned an invalid payload')`."
  **Actual:** `_staged_pdf_bytes` first coerces `bytearray` to `bytes` and returns it; only a payload that is neither `bytes` nor `bytearray` raises. Minor, but the failure mode as written would misdescribe a bytearray-returning storage client. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/ingestion.py:56-62`)
- **Wrong:** Step 24: each batch is "additionally wrapped in `retry_transient(attempts=3, base_delay=0.5s)`".
  **Actual:** Two imprecisions. The kwarg is `base_delay_seconds`, and the retry is not an outer wrapper — it is *inside* the callable handed to `gemini_pool.run`, so it re-runs per hop of the key chain. A capacity failure therefore burns 3 attempts on the primary key, then 3 more on each reserve key, not 3 in total. (The trace's own failureModes entry gets this ordering right; step 24 reads as if the retry wraps the whole pool call.) (`rag-thesis-backend/services/embedder.py:28-35,46-55`)
- **Wrong:** failureModes: the expired-lease sweep sets "`failure_category='worker_lease_expired'`".
  **Actual:** It sets `failure_category = coalesce(failure_category, 'worker_lease_expired')`, so a job that already recorded a category from a prior `schedule_upload_retry` keeps that one. The sweep also carries `and cancel_requested_at is null`, as does the staging-timeout sweep (line 367), which the trace omits from step 5. (`rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:367,379,384`)
- **Wrong:** Step 19 anchors the cleaning order to `document_processor.py:177-234,237-292`.
  **Actual:** The order stated is right (detect repeated lines -> `_clean_page` -> `redact_pii` -> `_remove_excluded_sections_from_pages`, driven from lines 281-292), but `redact_pii` is at document_processor.py:123-131, outside both cited ranges. The range 177-234 covers `_detect_repeated_lines`, `_clean_page` and the *dead* `_remove_excluded_sections`. Also, step 19's excluded-heading list omits `biographical sketch` (line 72), and step 18 says `page.get_text()` where the code is `page.get_text().strip()` — the strip is what makes the `< 40` scanned-page test behave. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/document_processor.py:123-131,70-75,264`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- The `record_upload_job_event` trigger is a single if/elif chain that emits AT MOST ONE event per UPDATE statement, in priority order INSERT > cancel_requested > status > stage > cleanup_status, and falls through to a bare `return new` when none of them changed. So the bare 30-second keep-alive (which changes only lease_expires_at/heartbeat_at/updated_at) writes NO event, and a checkpoint that changes stage while status stays 'processing' writes 'stage_changed', not 'status_changed'. `last_event_at` in GET /upload/status therefore does not advance on every heartbeat, which matters for anyone reading it as liveness (rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:106-119).
- Step 34 lists only the provenance and chunk_count validations. `commit_paper_ingestion` runs two earlier gates first: a blank `title` raises 'Paper title is required', and a `p_chunks` that is not a non-empty JSON array raises 'At least one verified chunk is required' (rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260720_index_embedding_provenance.sql:123-129). Both are reachable — `paper_data['title']` is `str(payload.get('title') or '')` with no server-side non-empty check in the worker (rag-thesis-backend/services/ingestion.py:193).
- `claim_upload_job` validates its own arguments before any sweep: a blank worker id or `p_lease_seconds` outside 30..900 raises 'Invalid worker lease request'. The Python side passes `settings.ingestion_lease_seconds`, which pydantic already bounds to the same 30..900 (rag-thesis-backend/migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:357-359, rag-thesis-backend/config.py:126).
- When the scanner is unavailable the housekeeping tick registers the worker as `state='degraded'` (not 'idle'), which is what `evaluate_operations` later reads; the trace's scanner branch only mentions the skipped claim and the alert (rag-thesis-backend/workers/ingestion_worker.py:229).
- `fail_upload_job` also overwrites `message` with the fixed 'Ingestion failed.' while putting the public sentence in `error`. The trace quotes the `error` text everywhere but never the message, which is what the timeline's stage row renders (rag-thesis-backend/supabase_setup.sql:1652-1653).
- There is a second, batch status surface the trace skips: `GET /upload/jobs?ids=` (`list_upload_jobs`) with `_last_event_map`, which does the same read for many jobs in one round trip and drops non-UUID ids in `_parse_job_ids` (rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:1098-1114,1163,1189).
- Verification of the trace's own test claim: `patch_pipeline` really does leave `refresh_after_ingest` unpatched, `conftest` sets SUPABASE_URL to the fake host, and nothing in conftest blocks sockets or stubs `services.retriever.sb` — conftest is 88 lines with no autouse network fixture. The outbound attempt is swallowed by `refresh_after_ingest`'s blanket except. Confirmed, not refuted (rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_durable_ingestion.py:146-161,169; rag-thesis-backend/tests/conftest.py:29).
- Every dead-code claim checks out against a grep of routers/, services/, workers/, scripts/ and evaluation/: `extract_pdf_text` (document_processor.py:295), `extract_text` (310), `filter_noise_chunks` (315), `_remove_excluded_sections` (217), `chunker.split_text` (chunker.py:180-181) and `novelty.meets_duplication_threshold` (novelty.py:109-111) have no non-test caller. `heartbeat_job` is called only from the PGRST202 fallback (upload_queue.py:68), and `is_retryable_ingestion_error`'s `LeaseLostError` arm is genuinely unreachable because `process_claimed_job` catches `LeaseLostError` at ingestion_worker.py:184 before the generic handler at 186.
- Anchor spot-checks that are exact and worth recording as verified: supabase_setup.sql:1511 (`next_retry_at = now()` on queue), supabase_setup.sql:513,519 (the always-NULL `content` term) and 1188-1196 (its comment), reserve_upload_job's INSERT column list at 1470-1479 omitting thesis_category/classification_status, tests/test_schema_consistency.py:140 (`set(base) & set(newest)`), migrations/20260724_operations_security.sql:37-41 (events FK `on delete set null`), migrations/20260819_thesis_category.sql:47-64 (hydrate trigger), services/cleanup.py:8-34, services/gemini_pool.py:228 and 270-282, services/retriever.py:764-817, routers/upload.py:497-511 and 1135-1137, Upload.jsx:305-307,314-333,337-350, PipelineTimeline.jsx:159-164, IngestOutcome.jsx:160-172. None of these needed correcting.

## 7. Batch upload and admin job monitoring

<a id="7-batch-upload"></a>

Flow key: `batch-upload`

### Overview

Batch upload of many PDFs and admin job monitoring — end to end, from Dropzone through the durable worker to the SQL commit and the two admin surfaces.

Shape of the thing, up front, because three of the task's premises do not match the code:

1. There is **no client-side concurrency control**. `uploadBatch` (rag-thesis-frontend/src/api.js:298) posts *all* files in ONE multipart request. The only concurrency anywhere in this flow is (a) `asyncio.Semaphore(3)` over Gemini completions in metadata extraction (rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:1321,1535) and (b) `settings.ingestion_concurrency`, which **defaults to 1** (rag-thesis-backend/config.py:136) — while the batch page's headline literally reads "Concurrent Workers Active" (rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/UploadBatch.jsx:680). Staging inside `upload_batch` is explicitly sequential (rag-thesis-backend/routers/upload.py:1043).
2. **`PipelineTimeline` is never rendered by the batch page.** It is imported only by the single-upload wizard (rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Upload.jsx:29,106). The batch page has its own, incompatible stage vocabulary (`STAGE_LABELS`, rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/upload/batchState.js:361).
3. **`UploadHistoryTab` does not show upload jobs.** It queries `listPapers` (rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/admin/UploadHistoryTab.jsx:39-42) — committed `papers` rows only. It shows no job, no stage, no failure, no cancellation. The only job view is `OperationsTab`, which is **superadmin-only** (rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Admin.jsx:133-135). A department admin who runs a batch has no server-side job history at all once the tab is closed.

**Trigger:** An authenticated user whose role has the `upload` feature (or any admin/superadmin) navigates to `/upload/batch` (rag-thesis-frontend/src/App.jsx:57-60, gated by `ProtectedRoute isAllowed={canUpload}`) and drops/selects up to 20 PDFs on the Dropzone, then presses "Continue" → "Ingest N manuscripts".

**Actors, in order:** React UploadBatch.jsx page + batchState reducer (browser) -> axios instance in src/api.js (JWT attach, 401 refresh, 502/504 GET retry) -> Vite dev proxy / nginx (HTML-aware bypass on the /upload prefix) -> BodySizeLimitMiddleware (ASGI, pre-auth) -> slowapi limiter (10/minute on the upload family, 120/minute global default) -> dependencies/auth.require_upload_access + resolve_effective_department -> routers/upload.py (extract-metadata batch, /upload/batch, /upload/jobs, /upload/jobs/{id}/cancel) -> services/catalog.resolve_academic_selection (via _ClassificationCache) -> Supabase Storage private 'pdfs' bucket -> PostgreSQL RPCs: reserve_upload_job, queue_upload_job, claim_upload_job, heartbeat_upload_job_control, commit_upload_ingestion, commit_paper_ingestion, request_upload_cancellation, finalize_upload_cancellation, fail_upload_job, schedule_upload_retry, claim_upload_cleanup, expire_upload_jobs -> record_upload_job_event trigger + hydrate_paper_academic_classification trigger -> workers/ingestion_worker.py (separate process, LeaseHeartbeat + optional ThreadPoolExecutor) -> services/ingestion.process_ingestion_job (download -> scan -> extract -> chunk -> embed -> screen -> index) -> ClamAV (clamd INSTREAM), Gemini embeddings + chat, PyMuPDF/tesserocr -> services/operations.evaluate_operations (alerts, worker registry) -> admin/OperationsTab (superadmin) and admin/UploadHistoryTab (papers, not jobs)

**Latency:** POST /upload/batch is one synchronous request that stages every file in series, so its wall time is N x (read + PyMuPDF open + sha256 + reserve RPC + storage PUT + queue RPC). The client allows 15 minutes for it (api.js:308); the server has no matching deadline. Every blocking step is on asyncio.to_thread so the event loop stays free, but the request itself holds a connection for the whole batch. POST /upload/batch/extract-metadata is bounded by three concurrent Gemini calls (_EXTRACT_CONCURRENCY = 3) over however many files the local title-page pass could not fully resolve; client timeout 10 minutes. Files whose title page yields all four fields cost no model call at all. Actual ingestion throughput is serial by default: ingestion_concurrency = 1, so a 20-file batch is 20 sequential pipelines. Claim latency is ingestion_poll_seconds = 2s per job. Within a job, the screen stage is the outlier — screen_new_submission issues one match_chunks RPC per chunk (novelty.py:352-359), so a 400-chunk thesis makes 400 round trips while progress sits frozen at 72 and only the 30s keep-alive touches the row. Lease geometry: lease 120s, heartbeat 30s, and config.py:244 asserts heartbeat*2 < lease. A stage that runs longer than 120s without the keep-alive thread landing would lose the lease; the keep-alive is what makes the long embed/screen stages survivable. Polling cost: the page polls GET /upload/jobs every 2s with all active ids in one request (30/min against the 120/min global default, which is why per-job polling was rejected — routers/upload.py:1190-1194). Each tick performs two reads: the jobs select, and _last_event_map, which pulls EVERY upload_job_events row for those ids with no LIMIT and discards all but the newest per job in Python (routers/upload.py:1098-1114). For a 20-file batch mid-flight that is hundreds of rows every two seconds, for one presentational timestamp the batch page never renders. Poll stops: 5 consecutive failures, or a hard 30-minute ceiling (POLL_CEILING_MS). Both are UI-side only; the worker keeps running. Admin monitoring cadence: OperationsTab polls jobs every 15s and summary/workers/alerts every 30s; evaluate_operations scans up to 500 job rows and 50 worker rows per call. The worker only runs it on its own 300s maintenance tick when OPERATIONS_MONITOR_ENABLED (default false), so in the default configuration alerts are computed lazily, only when a superadmin has the tab open.

### Steps (56)

| # | Layer | What happens | Anchor |
|---|---|---|---|

FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED LIMIT 1 over (queued \| retry_wait due \| processing with expired lease) AND cancel_requested_at IS NULL AND attempt_count<max_attempts AND source_stored, ordered by coalesce(next_retry_at, created_at). | 32 | worker | LeaseHeartbeat context manager. A _rpc_lock serialises the two so a keep-alive cannot land out of order; a _state_lock guards valid/cancel_requested. update() raises CancellationRequested when the control RPC reports cancel_requested, and returns False (marking the lease invalid) on any RPC exception. | `rag-thesis-backend/workers/ingestion_worker.py:60` |
