### Branches

- Admin route, MFA not enrolled: adminSecurityState -> 'setup' renders 'Secure administrator access' and NOTHING is fetched, regardless of the server's REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA (which defaults to False). Admin.jsx:51 gates the whole console on the client's own factor list.
- Admin route, enrolled but aal1: 'challenge' -> reportPrivilegedMfaRequired() raises PrivilegedMfaGate inline rather than sending the user to /login (Admin.jsx:80 explains the loop that would cause).
- E2E mode: adminSecurityState short-circuits to 'ready' (isE2ETestMode), the MFA query is disabled, and api.js attaches a literal 'Bearer deterministic-e2e-token'.
- superadmin vs admin in /analytics/overview: scope=None means unfiltered totals and unfiltered chat_query/chat_session counts; an admin gets everything narrowed to their profile department (analytics.py:214, :272).
- Pre-migration schema: /analytics/overview retries papers without thesis_category only on 42703 and returns an empty per_category Counter, which hides the 'Archive by category' card. /papers and /analytics/me have their own legacy-field fallbacks.
- OperationsTab visibility: the tab only exists when isSuperadmin (Admin.jsx:133) and every /maintenance route is require_superadmin, so an ordinary admin gets 403 'Superadmin privileges are required for this action.' if they call them directly.
- Backup monitoring: BACKUP_RPO_HOURS<=0 adds no condition AND resolves none, so backup_stale can never appear or clear; >0 with no backup_runs row yields the most severe form (critical, recorded_backups: 0); >0 with a row compares age_hours against the RPO.
- Malware scan disabled: 'disabled' joins the allowed scanner states, so a worker with ClamAV off is healthy rather than raising scanner_unavailable (operations.py:284).
- Alert lifecycle: an active condition upserts and keeps an existing 'acknowledged' status; a previously 'resolved' alert reopens as 'open', clears acknowledged_at/_by and increments occurrence_count by exactly 1.
- Webhook: not configured -> notify_webhook returns False immediately; 4xx other than 408/429 -> no retry; any final failure raises the 'webhook_degraded' alert, success resolves it.
- Role-feature read: get_role_features returns {} on failure. upload/novelty deny (fail closed); chat/archive read through DEFAULT_FEATURES and only an explicitly stored false denies.
- Department edit: the read comes from /catalog/departments/legacy, which on a PI-04 project derives tracks from programs/specializations and hard-codes track_label; on a pre-PI-04 project it falls back to _legacy_catalog() and returns the stored departments.tracks/track_label.
- Delete user with a blocking FK: 409 rather than 500 — but with the current migration set (20260803 changed upload_jobs.owner_id from RESTRICT to SET NULL) no auth.users FK is RESTRICT, so this branch is unreachable unless 20260723 is replayed after 20260803.
- Storage cleanup retry: a task already 'completed' returns 200 {status:'completed'} without touching storage; a task row that does not exist 404s via .limit(1) rather than .single() (the comment at maintenance.py:158 records exactly why).

### Failure modes

- /analytics/overview: any of the three paged reads throwing -> HTTP 503 'Administration analytics are temporarily unavailable.' React Query does not retry a 503 (main.jsx:46), so the Overview shows the flame-bordered banner 'Some administration data could not be loaded. No missing values are being treated as measured zeros.' with a Retry button that refetches only the failed query.
- /analytics/overview: the activity_log or chat_sessions COUNT failing does NOT 503 — _count logs a warning and returns 0, so 'AI queries' and 'Chat sessions' render a fabricated 0 with no banner. This directly contradicts the comment above it at analytics.py:236.
- /analytics/summary (public landing): any read failing -> 503 'Archive statistics are temporarily unavailable.' by design, so an unmeasured 0 is never published.
- /analytics/activity and /analytics/logs/system: unguarded reads — a Supabase failure escapes as an unhandled 500. The UI shows the activity error banner / 'System logs could not be loaded.' + Retry.
- /analytics/users: also unguarded; a failed read 500s and SystemManagementTab renders TableStateRow's errorLabel 'The user directory could not be loaded.' (error outranks empty, so it can never read 'No users found.').
- PUT users/{id}/role: 400 'You cannot change your own role.', 404 'User not found' (also for a malformed uuid via identifier_not_found), 403 'You can only modify users in your own department.' / 'Administrators cannot assign or modify superadmins.' Frontend toasts 'Role change failed' with apiErrorMessage.
- DELETE users/{id}: 400 self-delete, 404, 403 cross-department/superadmin, 409 dependent records, 500 'The user could not be deleted safely'. Toast 'Delete failed'.
- PUT users/{id}/details: adds 422 'Unknown department' and 403 'Administrators cannot reassign users to a different department.'; a blank full_name is a pydantic 422 before the handler runs — which is why the client substitutes displayNameFor().
- PUT /settings/features: 422 'Feature settings must define student and faculty roles' / 'Invalid feature settings for {role}' / 'Feature settings must use boolean values'. The optimistic setQueryData is rolled back by invalidateQueries and a toast 'Failed to update feature' appears — but the toggle visibly flips back only after the refetch lands.
- /maintenance/* reads: each wraps its own read and answers 503 with its own string ('Operational status is temporarily unavailable', 'Worker registry is temporarily unavailable', 'Upload operations are temporarily unavailable', 'Operational alerts are temporarily unavailable', 'Retention reporting is temporarily unavailable'). OperationsTab collapses the first four into one card: 'Operations data is unavailable. Confirm the operations migration is applied.' + Retry.
- POST /maintenance/alerts/{id}/acknowledge: 404 'Open operational alert not found' for missing, malformed, or already-resolved. Any other exception -> 503 'Operational alert could not be acknowledged' — AFTER the UPDATE has already committed, because record_security_event sits inside the same try (maintenance.py:100-117). Toast: 'Could not acknowledge alert'.
- POST /maintenance/retention/run?apply=true: 409 'Retention enforcement requires institutional approval and server enablement' when disabled. If the RPC succeeds but record_security_event then throws, the whole call returns 503 'Retention reporting is temporarily unavailable' even though rows were permanently deleted and NO audit row exists.
- POST /maintenance/storage-cleanup/{id}/retry: 404 'Cleanup task not found'; on a storage/DB failure it bumps attempts + error_category and raises 503 'Private-storage cleanup is still pending'. The partial-success window (bucket object removed, papers row delete then fails) leaves error_category set and status still 'pending'.
- notify_webhook failing three times: logged, a 'webhook_degraded' warning alert is upserted, evaluate_operations continues; if even that upsert fails it is logged and swallowed.
- upsert_alert fallback path (RPC missing, PGRST202/42883): it INSERTs into operational_alerts directly — but 20260724_operations_security.sql:530-536 revokes all and grants service_role only SELECT and UPDATE on that table, so the fallback insert is a privilege error, not a degradation.
- Worker registry RPCs missing: register_worker/stop_worker return False on PGRST202/42883 instead of raising, so a partially migrated project silently reports zero workers -> worker_unavailable critical.
- get_role_features failing: logged via logger.exception and returns {}; upload/novelty deny, chat/archive keep working on DEFAULT_FEATURES.
- 401 anywhere in the console: api.js attempts exactly one silent refreshSessionOnce() and replays; if that fails it signs out and hard-navigates to loginPathWithNext(current). 502/504 GETs are retried twice in the interceptor and then NOT retried again by React Query.
- 403 carrying PRIVILEGED_MFA_REQUIRED_DETAIL: the axios interceptor calls reportPrivilegedMfaRequired() so the shell raises the second-factor gate, and the promise still rejects so the calling screen also shows its own error.

### What gets persisted

- profiles: role/status (PUT users/{id}/role), full_name/role/department/status (PUT users/{id}/details). Both then invalidate the process-local role cache.
- auth.users: row removed by sb.auth.admin.delete_user; cascades profiles, chat_sessions, scan_history; nulls papers.uploaded_by, activity_log.user_id, upload_jobs.owner_id.
- activity_log: one row per role_change, user_delete, settings_update, storage_cleanup_completed (department resolved from the actor's profile when not supplied; failures are swallowed by log_activity).
- system_settings key='role_features': `value` replaced wholesale with the validated 2×4 matrix. `updated_at` is NOT written and there is no trigger, so it stays at the seed timestamp forever.
- operational_alerts: upserted by dedupe_key on every evaluate_operations pass (status/severity/safe_details/occurrence_count/last_seen_at/acknowledged_*), resolved when the condition clears, last_notified_at stamped by notify_webhook, acknowledged_at/_by/status by the acknowledge route.
- security_audit_events: rows for 'operational_alert_acknowledged', 'retention_run' (with applied + the counts), 'upload_cancellation'. Nothing in the API or UI ever reads this table.
- upload_job_events: written by the record_upload_job_event trigger on every upload_jobs insert/status/stage/cleanup change — not by the console, but it is the table the retention counts and the 90-day window act on.
- backup_runs: one upserted row per successful backup, written only by the record_backup_run RPC called from PowerShell on the backup machine.
- storage_cleanup_queue: rows inserted by services/cleanup.record_storage_cleanup when a paper's private file cannot be removed; the maintenance retry route flips status/attempts/error_category and clears the owning upload_jobs row's cleanup_status.
- upload_jobs: cleanup_status/source_stored set by the storage-cleanup retry route.
- departments: name/track_label/tracks written by PUT /departments/{id}; rows deleted by DELETE /departments/{id}.
- DELETES on apply=true retention: upload_job_events older than 90 days, resolved operational_alerts resolved >90 days ago, security_audit_events older than 1 year — irreversible, inside a security-definer function.

### Surprises

- _fetch_all pages with .range() but never applies an .order(). PostgREST/Postgres give no row order without ORDER BY, so beyond 1000 papers/profiles/scans the page boundaries are undefined and rows can be duplicated or skipped between pages. The exact `count` keeps the *totals* honest, which is precisely what hides the problem: per_track, per_year, total_chunks and avg_duplication can be wrong while the headline numbers look right. analytics.py:76.
- The module comment at analytics.py:236 says an unavailable read 'declines rather than reporting a fabricated zero', and then the very next block uses the tolerant _count for usage.chat_queries and usage.chat_sessions, which returns 0 on any exception. The frontend banner ('No missing values are being treated as measured zeros') only fires on a query-level error, so a failed count renders a confident '0 AI queries' with no warning. analytics.py:120 vs :271.
- /maintenance/operations/summary is a GET that mutates: it upserts and resolves operational_alerts and can fire an outbound signed webhook. Leaving the Operations tab open in a browser drives the alert pipeline on a 30-second cadence, and a second tab (or the worker, or the API monitor thread) does it concurrently.
- security_audit_events is write-only. Three code paths insert into it (alert acknowledgement, retention runs, upload cancellations) and no endpoint, router or UI ever reads it. The 'Raw System Logs' pane reads activity_log, which those three paths do not write — so the console's audit trail does not contain the console's own most sensitive actions.
- Retention 'apply' has no client. api.js exposes only getRetentionReport (GET .../report); POST /maintenance/retention/run?apply=true — the only endpoint that deletes audit history — is reachable only by hand-crafted request. Same for GET /maintenance/storage-cleanup and its {id}/retry.
- The Operations 'Pending cleanup' tile does NOT count storage_cleanup_queue. It counts upload_jobs rows whose cleanup_status is pending/processing and stale (operations.py:306). The storage_cleanup_queue table — which holds the failed paper-file deletions — has no console surface at all.
- OperationsTab renders 4 of the 10 fields the summary returns. backup_monitored and backup_stale in particular are computed, returned, and thrown away; backup staleness is visible only if you notice a 'backup stale' row in the alerts list. docs/BACKUP_RESTORE_DRILL.md:91 tells the operator to verify those two fields via the raw endpoint, because the UI does not show them.
- acknowledge_alert and run_retention both put record_security_event INSIDE the try that maps to 503. The mutation has already committed when the audit write fails, so the operator is told the action failed while it actually succeeded — and for retention that means rows are permanently deleted with no audit record and a 503 response.
- The Departments editor round-trips through two different shapes. It READS /catalog/departments/legacy, which on a PI-04 project overwrites track_label with the literal 'Program / specialization' and derives `tracks` from programs/specializations (catalog.py:109-113), and WRITES departments.track_label/tracks (departments.py:148-151). So saving an unedited row silently overwrites the stored legacy columns with catalog-derived values, and because the refetch re-derives the same strings the UI looks correct either way. The 'Track Label' input can never display what is actually stored.
- That same department list comes from an UNAUTHENTICATED, publicly rate-limited endpoint (catalog.py:187), even though the editing actions beside it are superadmin-only.
- The /admin MFA gate is entirely client-side and unconditional. REQUIRE_PRIVILEGED_MFA defaults to False (config.py:113), so the server happily serves an aal1 admin — but Admin.jsx:51 still refuses to render the console until supabase.auth.mfa reports a verified TOTP factor at aal2. The frontend is stricter than the backend here, and the enrolment nag appears even on a deployment that never enabled the setting.
- invalidate_features_cache(), invalidate_role_cache() and the _CONTRACT_CACHE are all plain module dicts. With more than one uvicorn worker, a feature toggle or role change is enforced immediately only in the process that handled the write; the others keep the old policy for up to 60 s. The realtime broadcast fixes the *browser* instantly, which makes the server-side lag easy to miss.
- require_upload_access and require_novelty_access do not call _feature_enabled. They re-implement the lookup inline (auth.py:428, :445) without the DEFAULT_FEATURES fallback — deliberate (they must fail closed), but it means the function whose docstring explains the whole policy is used by only two of the four guards.
- PUT /analytics/users/{id}/details returns result.data[0] straight from PostgREST, i.e. the full profiles row with no `select=`. The module went to the trouble of pinning _PROFILE_FIELDS and _ACTIVITY_FIELDS exactly to stop future columns leaking (analytics.py:30-38), and the update endpoint bypasses that.
- evaluate_operations treats every status='failed' job as 'retries exhausted' and files it under the dedupe key 'retries_exhausted'. The comment at operations.py:311 explains this is intentional (a malware/encrypted manuscript fails on attempt 1 of 3), but the alert name and the summary field failed_jobs therefore do not mean what they say.
- The 409 branch in delete_user is effectively dead against the current schema: migration 20260803 changed the only RESTRICT foreign key to auth.users (upload_jobs.owner_id) to SET NULL. It becomes live again only if 20260723 is replayed after 20260803 — exactly the create-or-replace/replay hazard CLAUDE.md warns about.
- upsert_alert's no-RPC fallback INSERTs into operational_alerts, but the migration grants service_role only SELECT and UPDATE on that table (20260724_operations_security.sql:534-536). The compatibility path cannot work as written.
- UNCOMMITTED AdminOverview.jsx: the block comment at :186-199 still says 'Still deliberately no figures' and 'the border and icon moved to the neutral forest tone', immediately above ~85 lines that render a full figures table including a gold badge. The first half of the comment describes the code that was deleted; only the last two sentences describe what is there. docs/DEFENSE_WALKTHROUGH.md was updated in the same working tree and correctly describes the new behaviour, so the comment is the stale artefact.
- The same uncommitted change adds a static import of src/data/objective2Summary.json into AdminOverview — an untracked directory. If that file is missing the admin overview chunk fails to build, and tests/test_objective2_summary_export.py skips silently rather than failing when either the source run or the generated file is absent (test file:30).
- fmtP prints p-values below 0.001 in exponential form and everything else to 4dp, while scores print to 3dp — a deliberate mismatch documented at AdminOverview.jsx:28 because the export and iso25010_evidence.md disagree in the 4th decimal on the two n<5 strata. The walkthrough calls that discrepancy 'unexplained and worth chasing after the defense'.
- The Overview's ACTION_LABELS covers five action types; user_delete, settings_update, storage_cleanup_completed and paper_delete_pending fall through and render their raw snake_case action name in the activity feed.

**Adversarial fact-check:** verdict `mostly-accurate`, 9 correction(s), 9 omission(s). Corrections are already folded into the text above. They are kept because each records a wrong belief a competent reader reached from the same files - the same trap is available to you.

- **Wrong:** OperationsTab collapses the first four failed queries into one card reading 'Operations data is unavailable. Confirm the operations migration is applied.'
  **Actual:** That string is only the `apiErrorMessage` FALLBACK. `apiErrorMessage` returns `error.response.data.detail` first (rag-thesis-frontend/src/lib/apiErrors.js:11-15), and every /maintenance 503 carries one, so the card actually renders the backend string ('Operational status is temporarily unavailable', 'Worker registry is temporarily unavailable', …). With no response at all it returns `error.message` (apiErrors.js:18-20), and a shape failure returns 'Invalid operations <field> response from the backend.' (rag-thesis-frontend/src/api.js:390). The migration-hint fallback is effectively unreachable. (`rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/admin/OperationsTab.jsx:91`)
- **Wrong:** The retention card shows the three counts as neutral badges, or an explicit unavailable message + Retry on isError — deliberately not `?? 0`.
  **Actual:** The success path does use `?? 0` on all three badges. Only the isError branch avoids it. The comment at OperationsTab.jsx:148-152 says exactly that: `?? 0` was wrong for the *unreachable-endpoint* case, and the fix was the isError branch, not removing `?? 0`. (`rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/admin/OperationsTab.jsx:162`)
- **Wrong:** 'a single poll can block for ~16 s worst case' (three webhook attempts, 0/0.25/1.0 s sleeps, 5 s timeout each).
  **Actual:** `notify_webhook` is called per-alert inside the condition loop, not once per poll. With all 7 conditions active and all past the 15-minute `_notify_due` window, that is up to 7 × (1.25 s of sleeps + 3 × OPERATIONS_ALERT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS) ≈ 114 s inside one synchronous GET, plus a `webhook_degraded` upsert after each failure. The 180 s axios timeout is the only real ceiling. (`rag-thesis-backend/services/operations.py:352`)
- **Wrong:** Step 47 anchors GET /maintenance/alerts at maintenance.py:97.
  **Actual:** maintenance.py:97 is the acknowledge POST decorator. `list_operational_alerts` is at :85-94. Also the acknowledge UPDATE writes four fields, not three — `updated_at` is set alongside status/acknowledged_at/acknowledged_by. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/maintenance.py:85`)
- **Wrong:** latencyNotes: 'Opening the Overview issues three API calls that together do: 1 profile read for require_admin's role cache, 1 more in _admin_scope…'
  **Actual:** Undercounts by one uncached read plus a network hop per request. `get_current_user` calls `sb.auth.get_user` (remote round trip to Supabase) and then `_ensure_approved_account`, which is an UNCACHED `profiles.select('status')` read on every authenticated request — there is no cache on it, unlike `get_user_role`'s 60 s one. So each of the three Overview calls costs: 1 Supabase auth call + 1 uncached status read + (usually cached) role read + 1 `_admin_scope` read = 2 uncached profile reads each, 6 across the tab. (`rag-thesis-backend/dependencies/auth.py:141`)
- **Wrong:** failureModes: 'each /maintenance/* read wraps its own read and answers 503 with its own string'.
  **Actual:** `GET /maintenance/storage-cleanup` has no try/except at all. A Supabase failure there escapes as an unhandled 500, not a named 503 — it is the one /maintenance read that breaks the pattern. (It has no frontend caller either, which the trace does note.) (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/maintenance.py:144`)
- **Wrong:** Step 33 anchors the Select-gating rule ('only for other users, and only when isSuperadmin or the target is not admin/superadmin') at AdminOverview.jsx:118.
  **Actual:** AdminOverview.jsx:118 is `changeRole` (the `{role, status: selectedUser.status}` half of the step, which is correct at :125). The visibility condition `u.id !== me?.id && (isSuperadmin || !['admin','superadmin'].includes(u.role))` is at :361, and the superadmin-only extra <option> entries at :371-372. (`rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminOverview.jsx:361`)
- **Wrong:** Step 22 / branch: 'Archive by category' renders only when per_category is non-empty, i.e. the thesis_category migration is applied.
  **Actual:** The Counter is also empty when the scoped archive has zero ready papers — `_overview_papers` builds it by iterating the rows, so an empty department hides the card on a fully migrated project too. Migration state is not the only cause. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/analytics.py:193`)
- **Wrong:** surprises: ACTION_LABELS covers five action types; user_delete, settings_update, storage_cleanup_completed and paper_delete_pending fall through.
  **Actual:** Seven written actions fall through, not four. Add `duplication_chat_blocked` (routers/duplication.py:485), `scan_history_delete` (:599) and `scan_history_clear` (:607). All ten actions actually written are: chat_query, paper_upload, paper_delete, novelty_scan, role_change (mapped) + those seven. (`rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminOverview.jsx:42`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- MIDDLEWARE, entirely absent from the trace: `SlowAPIMiddleware` is registered with `default_limits=['120/minute']`, so every /analytics and /maintenance call is rate-limited and can return 429 before any guard runs. The key is `user:<sub>` when SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET is set, else a hashed IP+X-Guest-ID or bare IP. OperationsTab's four pollers alone contribute ~14 req/min per admin, and the manual Refresh fires five at once. main.py:155, rag-thesis-backend/services/rate_limiting.py:41-45.
- Also missing from the middleware stack: GZipMiddleware (minimum_size=1024, main.py:158), the `security_headers` HTTP middleware (nosniff / DENY / no-referrer / Permissions-Policy / HSTS on https, main.py:161-171), BodySizeLimitMiddleware (main.py:180) and CORSMiddleware registered LAST so it is outermost and 429/413 responses stay readable to the browser (main.py:187-201).
- Step 1 covers only ProtectedRoute's role check. It also gates on `loading` -> RouteSkeleton, `!user` -> /login, and crucially `needsMfa` -> <Navigate to="/login"> which runs BEFORE Admin.jsx's own MFA probe ever mounts; plus `profileError` -> a 'Profile temporarily unavailable' screen and `isPending` / `isRejected` screens with a Sign out button. rag-thesis-frontend/src/components/ProtectedRoute.jsx:17-27, :29, :47, :71.
- Step 53 omits the guard on the papers query: `listPapers(null)` hits GET /papers behind `require_archive_access` (ArchiveUser), not an admin guard — the only admin-console call that does not go through require_admin/require_superadmin. And for a non-superadmin the backend overwrites the department parameter with the caller's own profile department (defaulting to 'CCSICT'), which is why the 'All Depts' picker beside it is superadmin-only. rag-thesis-backend/routers/papers.py:40, :51-55.
- api.js wraps the four operations reads in `requireOperationsObject`, which throws a TypeError ('Invalid operations <field> response from the backend.') on a malformed shape before React Query ever sees the data — a client-side validation layer between the 503 handling and the UI. rag-thesis-frontend/src/api.js:390-409.
- SystemManagementTab's Approve/Reject path branches on the actor's role: a superadmin goes through PUT /analytics/users/{id}/details (full_name + role + department + status), an admin through PUT /analytics/users/{id}/role (role + status). Two different endpoints, two different validation sets, for the same button. rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/admin/SystemManagementTab.jsx:455-467.
- POST /maintenance/retention/run with apply=false ALSO writes a `retention_run` security_audit_events row (with applied:false and the counts). The trace says 'No audit row is written for the read-only path' — true for GET /retention/report, but the POST's dry-run path is audited. rag-thesis-backend/routers/maintenance.py:127.
- main.jsx:57 installs `queryClient.setQueryDefaults(['departments'], { select: normalizeDepartments })` while api.js:423 already calls `normalizeDepartments` on the same payload — the ['departments'] cache is normalized twice (idempotent, so harmless, but it means the query default is dead work for every caller that goes through getDepartments).
- resolve_alert carries a `.neq('status','resolved')` guard, so re-resolving an already-resolved alert writes nothing and does not churn `updated_at` — step 41 describes it as a bare UPDATE. rag-thesis-backend/services/operations.py:142.

## 12. Producing defense evidence: evaluation, fingerprint, load test, deck

<a id="12-evidence-pipeline"></a>

Flow key: `evidence-pipeline`

### Overview

Defense evidence production: Objective 2 evaluation run -> results artifacts -> UI summary export; release fingerprint; JMeter load tests -> summarizers; screenshot capture -> PPTX deck; and paper_ORIGINAL -> paper_CORRECTED.docx. Five chains that share exactly one artifact (rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/comparison_20260913_064053.json) and one prose file (rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md). None of them run in CI, and the paper carries none of the Objective 2 measured figures.

**Trigger:** An operator manually invokes one of five commands from a developer machine: `python -m evaluation.run_comparison [--fresh]`, `python -m scripts.release_fingerprint`, `java -jar ApacheJMeter.jar -n -t jmeter/<plan>.jmx` followed by `python -m evaluation.summarize_jmeter|summarize_chat_load`, `node docs/deck/capture.mjs` + `python -m docs.deck.build`, or `cd paper; python build_corrections.py`. Nothing is triggered by a push, a webhook, or a schedule: `.github/workflows/quality.yml` contains zero references to run_comparison, release_fingerprint, summarize_jmeter, docs/deck or build_corrections (verified by grep).

**Actors, in order:** Operator shell (PowerShell/bash, backend .venv) -> evaluation/run_comparison.py (harness process) -> routers/chat.py::_chat_impl (in-process, NOT over HTTP) -> services/retriever + gemini_pool + citations + chat_notices -> Supabase Postgres via match_chunks RPC -> Gemini API (chat gemini-3.6-flash, judge gemini-3.5-flash-lite, embed models/gemini-embedding-001) -> Ragas 0.4.3 via Gemini's OpenAI-compatible endpoint -> scripts/release_fingerprint.py::build_manifest -> scripts/export_objective2_summary.py -> rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminOverview.jsx -> Apache JMeter 5.6.3 (jar) + evaluation/summarize_jmeter.py + summarize_chat_load.py -> docs/deck/capture.mjs (Playwright from the frontend's node_modules) -> docs/deck/build.py + evidence.py + charts.py + orb.py + verify.py -> paper/build_corrections.py + paper/_docxlib.py

**Latency:** A full formal run is roughly 80 model calls for the pathways plus ~160 judged calls for scoring (two AnswerCorrectness + Faithfulness + ContextPrecision per row), which is why every query is checkpointed before the next begins (run_comparison.py:500-510). Retry backoff is 20/45/90 s, up to 4 attempts, applied independently to pathway attempts and to each judged() call. The measured RAG arm's own end-to-end latency is recorded per row as rag_end_to_end_latency_s; the committed /chat load evidence puts genuine answers at 5.1-26.2 s and capacity-cooldown notices at 1-18 ms, which is the empty gap the 100 ms / 2000 ms bands in summarize_chat_load.py:43-44 sit in. Application-only throughput with no provider in the path is p95 204 ms over 900 samples (provider_summary.json). The deck build downloads six TTFs on first run and rasterises 36 GIF frames plus stills unless they already exist (orb.render_all caches by file existence, force=False). capture.mjs sleeps 25 s between /chat captures to stay under the free-tier per-minute quota and allows up to 120 s for an answer to render.

### Steps (50)

| # | Layer | What happens | Anchor |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | external | Operator sets three env values before a formal run: LLM_BASE_URL unset, APP_ENVIRONMENT=development, GUEST_DAILY_TOKEN_BUDGET=0. These are documented only in prose; nothing in the harness asserts them. A gateway run is merely *recorded* (generation_route), not refused. | `README.md:308-312` |
| 2 | service | `python -m evaluation.run_comparison --fresh` enters main(). Module import already constructed the baseline model as a module-level singleton with the production settings (thinking_level, max_output_tokens, timeout), so importing the harness for a unit test also builds a live ChatGoogleGenerativeAI. | `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:827, :101-108` |
| 3 | service | Dataset JSON is loaded and validate_formal_dataset() returns a list of blocking conditions: 30-50 queries, unique ids, non-empty question, ground_truth/source_thesis not starting 'REPLACE:', corpus_coverage in ('present','absent_topic','absent_unreleased','absent_by_design','undetermined') but *not* 'undetermined', validated_by_faculty_panel is True, exactly three distinct panel members with ISO-8601 dates. Any issue -> parser.error() (exit 2) unless --allow-unvalidated. | `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:157-215, :864-873` |
| 4 | service | run_id = sha256_file(dataset)[:12] -> '5e8fb7f21db6'. Checkpoint paths become results/checkpoints/5e8fb7f21db6.{pathways,scores}.jsonl and .provenance.json. --fresh unlinks all three. | `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:884-892` |
| 5 | service | build_manifest() is called once, and its result is used for BOTH the resume guard and the published `reproducibility.release` block (the comment at :894-897 says two calls could disagree). | `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:898 -> scripts/release_fingerprint.py:50` |
| 6 | service | build_manifest() SHA-256s exactly 11 files: requirements.txt, requirements.lock, config.py, routers/chat.py, services/prompts.py, services/retriever.py, services/question_types.py, frontend package-lock.json, both Dockerfiles, docker-compose.operations.yml. It also records schema_version 4, git_commit, python version, the three model names, PROMPT_VERSION, generation_contract, generation_route (gateway_enabled/host/bounds/reserve_key_count), rag_contract (800/100/0.30/5/15/3/0.85/CCSICT) and index_fingerprint. | `rag-thesis-backend/scripts/release_fingerprint.py:51-141` |
| 7 | service | index_fingerprint comes from current_index_fingerprint(): embedding model, 768 dimensions, PREPROCESSING_VERSION 'document-v1', CHUNKING_VERSION 'token-v1', tokenizer 'cl100k_base', chunk sizes, provenance_status 'verified'. These are *string constants*, not hashes of the code that implements them. | `rag-thesis-backend/services/index_provenance.py:11-22` |
| 8 | service | guard_checkpoint_provenance(): with no checkpoint on disk it writes the 9-key answering_configuration to <run-id>.provenance.json and returns. With a checkpoint and no provenance file it parser.error()s. With both, configuration_differences() expands input_sha256 to the individual changed filename and parser.error()s listing every changed key. git_commit is deliberately excluded so a docs commit cannot refuse a resume. | `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:449-497, :409-425` |
| 9 | service | run_pathways() iterates queries serially, replaying any id already in the checkpoint file and otherwise calling _run_query(). | `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:500-525` |
| 10 | service | _run_query() calls chat_notices.reset_capacity_limit() BEFORE each attempt, otherwise a retry would instantly replay the 60 s process-wide cooldown notice rather than re-asking the provider. | `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:356` |
| 11 | service | _attempt_pathways() runs both arms concurrently with asyncio.gather. Baseline: gemini_pool.arun(baseline_llm, CHAT, ainvoke(BASELINE_PROMPT)) — routed through the key pool so reserve-key rotation is symmetric between arms. | `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:249-282, :110-114` |
| 12 | api | RAG arm calls routers.chat._chat_impl(ChatRequest(...), None, BackgroundTasks(), None, evaluation_trace=trace) DIRECTLY in-process. request=None and user=None, so it runs as a guest but bypasses the HTTP route entirely: no SlowAPI rate limit, no Turnstile, no guest-budget middleware, no CORS, no /chat router wrapper persistence. | `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:269-278` |

### Branches

- --fresh vs resume: without --fresh, existing checkpoints are replayed verbatim through _load_checkpoint(); guard_checkpoint_provenance compares 9 manifest keys and parser.error()s naming each changed key (input_sha256 expanded to the specific file). git_commit is excluded from the comparison on purpose (run_comparison.py:404-418).
- Resumed rows predating a schema change: rows with corpus_coverage outside DECLARABLE_COVERAGE are counted in `rows_without_corpus_coverage`, printed as a WARNING, and force formal_result false. Only reachable with an explicit --run-id, since the default run id is the dataset digest (run_comparison.py:906-920).
- --allow-unvalidated: prints 'WARNING: development-only run' and proceeds, and dataset_issues being non-empty forces formal_result false (run_comparison.py:873-874, :952).
- --skip-ragas: no `ragas`, `statistics`, `means`, `by_corpus_coverage` or `rag_diagnostics` keys are written at all, so downstream consumers (export_objective2_summary.build, deck evidence.load_comparison) raise KeyError on such a file (run_comparison.py:964).
- Capacity notice in the RAG arm: is_unattempted() true -> up to 4 attempts with 20/45/90 s backoff, each preceded by reset_capacity_limit(); recovery keeps the real answer, persistent failure builds a row with rag_unattempted true (run_comparison.py:347-390).
- Exception (not a notice) in either arm: same 4-attempt backoff, then _unattempted_row() with rag_kind None and a 300-char 'failure' string, and the run CONTINUES rather than dying (run_comparison.py:365-377).
- Rows with no retrieved contexts: Faithfulness and Context Precision are set to None and counted in `not_applicable`, while Answer Correctness is still scored (run_comparison.py:644-670). In the formal run not_applicable is 0 for both.
- Deck --allow-fixture-captures: Captures.get() raises SystemExit for any manifest entry whose mode != 'live'. The committed manifest marks `archive` and `novelty` as fixture, so a default `python -m docs.deck.build` DIES on slide 7 (docs/deck/build.py:102-104, docs/deck/assets/captures/manifest.json).
- Deck --allow-missing: a missing capture returns None and frame() draws a '[CAPTURE] <label>' placeholder; a missing ISO block prints a warning and slide 9 fills with '[VERIFY]'. verify.py --draft is then required to pass (docs/deck/build.py:96-100, :140-145, :237-244).
- Deck --motif logo: rebinds the module-level RENDERS global to tmp/deck/renders/<mode>-logo and logo_renders() raises SystemExit naming the two commands to run rather than falling back to the orb (docs/deck/build.py:107-133, :715-719).
- DECK_THEME=light|dark: chosen at theme import time; anything else raises SystemExit at import (docs/deck/theme.py:18-20).
- JMeter: /chat runs must go through summarize_chat_load, everything else through summarize_jmeter. Using the wrong one on a /chat JTL yields chat_summary_10u.json's '0.0% error rate, median 3 ms' — which the band report reveals as 0 answers and 29 capacity notices.
- summarize_jmeter --require-response-code: raises ValueError and writes NO output file if the code was never observed (evaluation/summarize_jmeter.py:77-82, :107-109).

### Failure modes

- Dataset incomplete or a stratum still 'undetermined' -> argparse parser.error() prints 'formal evaluation is blocked until the dataset is complete:' with a bulleted list and exits 2 (run_comparison.py:864-871).
- Checkpoint exists but provenance.json does not -> parser.error('<file> exists but <run-id>.provenance.json does not, so there is no record of the configuration those answers were produced under. Re-run with --fresh...'), exit 2 (run_comparison.py:479-484).
- Configuration drifted since the checkpoint -> parser.error('the configuration changed since ... Changed: - models - input_sha256.rag-thesis-backend/services/prompts.py ...Re-run with --fresh...'), exit 2 (run_comparison.py:485-497).
- Provider 429 during the run -> _chat_impl returns HTTP-200-shaped ChatResponse carrying CAPACITY_MESSAGE ('IskAI has reached the research AI service usage limit, so your question could not be processed right now. Please try again later.') and mark_capacity_limited() trips a 60 s cooldown; the harness retries 4x then records rag_unattempted, prints 'WARNING: N of M queries never reached the provider and are excluded from the paired test: [ids]', and sets formal_result false (chat_notices.py:48-51, :218-221; run_comparison.py:922-928).
- Retrieval itself raises a non-capacity error inside _chat_impl -> HTTPException 503 'The thesis archive is temporarily unavailable. Please try again in a moment.' The harness catches it as a generic Exception and retries (routers/chat.py:2629-2636).
- Judge call truncated mid-JSON -> instructor IncompleteOutputException. This was a deterministic failure that burned all 4 attempts and killed scoring after every answer was already paid for; fixed by _EVALUATOR_MAX_TOKENS=16384 (run_comparison.py:544-558).
- A judged() call still failing after 4 attempts re-raises and aborts the whole scoring pass; the pathways checkpoint survives, the scores checkpoint holds only completed rows (run_comparison.py:603-613).
- json.dumps(..., allow_nan=False) raises ValueError if any metric is NaN, so a NaN score fails the write instead of emitting invalid JSON (run_comparison.py:1014-1017).
- pandas absent -> CSV silently not written, no message (run_comparison.py:1020-1024).
- export_objective2_summary with a missing run -> stderr 'missing source run: <path>', exit 1. With a missing checkpoint provenance -> stderr 'no checkpoint for run id 5e8fb7f21db6: <path>', exit 1 (export_objective2_summary.py:127-135).
- objective2Summary.json hand-edited -> test_committed_summary_matches_the_source_run fails with 'src/data/objective2Summary.json has drifted from the run. Regenerate with python -m scripts.export_objective2_summary' (test_objective2_summary_export.py:47-50).
- The `present` stratum becoming significant in a future run -> test_the_quoted_stratum_is_present_and_not_significant fails, forcing Section 3.2.5 to be rewritten in the same change (test_objective2_summary_export.py:78-93).
- Deck: a text box that cannot hold its content -> pptx_helpers.Overflow, collected into ISSUES, printed as 'slide N: <sample> needs X in, box is Y in' and SystemExit(1) (docs/deck/build.py:673-681, pptx_helpers.py:183-185).
- Deck: content past CONTENT_BOTTOM 7.05 in -> check() appends '<what> ends at X in, past 7.05 in' and the same SystemExit(1) (docs/deck/build.py:78-80).
- Deck: _self_check inconsistency in the run file -> ValueError 'mean of per-row deltas does not equal the recorded mean difference' / 'stratum sizes do not sum to the pooled n' / 'not a formal, fully scored run' / "unexpected rag_kind 'x' on row N" (docs/deck/evidence.py:154-163).
- Deck: a figure quoted on a slide but absent from the evidence file -> ValueError 'quoted on a slide but absent from the evidence file: <list>' (docs/deck/evidence.py:234-239).
- Deck: fonts endpoint unreachable -> urllib raises inside ensure_fonts(); matplotlib would otherwise silently fall back to DejaVu, which is why it is fetched rather than assumed (docs/deck/fonts.py:27-52).
- Paper: any correction whose needle no longer matches exactly N times -> AssertionError '<label>: expected N, matched M' and the whole build aborts before writing DST, so paper_CORRECTED.docx is never left half-applied (paper/_docxlib.py:212-213).
- Chat-load evidence misread: a /chat JTL of 100% HTTP 200 with a 3 ms median describes a system that answered nothing. chat_summary_20u.json reports error_rate_percent 0.0 and 60x200; chat_rag_load_report.json's 20_users profile reports answer: 0, capacity_notice: 60, answered_share_percent 0.0.

### What gets persisted

- rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/checkpoints/<run-id>.pathways.jsonl — one JSON line per query, UNSANITIZED (carries rag_context and rag_contexts, i.e. archived manuscript text). 1.8 MB for the formal run. Gitignored, never committed.
- rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/checkpoints/<run-id>.scores.jsonl — {id, baseline:{answer_correctness}, rag:{answer_correctness, faithfulness, context_precision}}. Gitignored.
- rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/checkpoints/<run-id>.provenance.json — the 9-key answering configuration (no git_commit). Gitignored, yet export_objective2_summary.main() refuses to run without it.
- rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/comparison_<stamp>.json — the published, sanitized run. Directory is gitignored; three files are force-added (comparison_20260728_140718.json, comparison_20260913_064053.json/.csv). Eight other comparison_*.json sit on disk untracked.
- rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/chunk_size_sweep_2026-09-14.json — the artifact the paper's chunk-size claim rests on. Gitignored and NOT committed.
- rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/jmeter/*.jtl and *.json — all force-added despite the directory ignore.
- tmp/chunk_sweep_embeddings.jsonl — the embedding cache the sweep depends on; scratch, never committed.
- rag-thesis-frontend/src/data/objective2Summary.json — committed by design so the frontend builds without the harness. Currently UNTRACKED in the working tree (git status: '?? rag-thesis-frontend/src/data/').
- docs/deck/assets/captures/*.png + manifest.json + archive_state.json — committed; the manifest records mode/theme/captured_at/head/sha256 per capture.
- tmp/deck/captures/<mode>-<theme>/ — raw captures, scratch.
- tmp/deck/renders/<mode>/ and tmp/deck/fonts/ — orb stills, turntable GIF, glow, forest.png, deltas.png, downloaded TTFs. Scratch.
- tmp/deck/out/IskAI_Defense_Deck.pptx + .build.json sidecar (built_at, theme, head, objective2_run, iso_block, captures used, morph plan, render sha256[:16], size_bytes). Scratch — docs/deck/.gitignore is just '__pycache__/', but OUT_DEFAULT points into tmp/.
- paper/paper_CORRECTED.docx — committed, 6.3 MB, regenerated from paper_ORIGINAL_2026-08-09.docx on every run.
- paper/paper_CORRECTED.pdf — committed, 1.5 MB, dated a day BEFORE the .docx; no script produces it.
- tmp/defense/Chapter1_Defense_Packet.pdf — scratch.
- docs/evidence/OBJECTIVE_2_COMPARISON_2026-09-13.html — committed, 49 KB, NO generator in the repository.

### Surprises

- **The paper contains none of the Objective 2 results.** build_corrections.py writes zero measured figures from comparison_20260913_064053.json. The PROSE12 comment states it outright: 'Deliberately carries no measured value from the 2026-09-13 run: this is a proposal, the paper ends at Chapter 3.' The only measured numbers the paper carries are (a) dependency and tool versions from requirements.lock / package.json / Dockerfile / quality.yml, (b) 'SonarQube Community Build 26.7.0.124771' and 'SonarScanner CLI v8.0.1.6346' from the 2026-09-01 scan, (c) '96.30% matched-chunk coverage at 94.94% highest passage similarity' from iso25010_evidence.md:272 (build_corrections.py:774-776), and (d) the frozen RAG constants 0.30 / top-k 5 / 800 / 100 / 85% / 50% / 99.9%, which come from config.py. Everything a reader would call 'the result' lives only in the JSON, the evidence md, the HTML and the admin card.
- **The chunk-size claim in the paper cites an artifact that is not in the repository.** The correction at build_corrections.py:129-137 asserts 'retrieval quality statistically indistinguishable across 400 to 1,000 tokens'; the supporting hit@5 68.75-75.00% / MRR 0.533-0.604 numbers appear ONLY in a Python comment (build_corrections.py:123). The named source, evaluation/results/chunk_size_sweep_2026-09-14.json, is gitignored and uncommitted, and its embedding cache lives in tmp/. A reader cannot verify the claim from a clone.
- **A default deck build fails.** docs/deck/assets/captures/manifest.json marks `archive` and `novelty` as mode 'fixture' (fabricated e2e data). Captures.get() raises SystemExit for any non-live entry, and slide 7 requests both (build.py:482-483). So `python -m docs.deck.build` with no flags dies unless --allow-fixture-captures or --allow-missing is passed — meaning the deck as buildable today puts fabricated /archive and /novelty screenshots on the objective-3 slide.
- **release_fingerprint.py has no output artifact.** Its --output flag is never used by any script, runbook or CI job, and `find docs/evidence -type f` returns no fingerprint JSON. The only place a manifest is actually persisted is embedded as `reproducibility.release` inside the comparison JSON, produced by the harness's own build_manifest() call (run_comparison.py:898).
- **The fingerprint does not hash the code that decides whether an answer survives.** The 11 hashed inputs exclude services/citations.py, services/chunker.py and services/guards.py. The 2026-09-12 audit note says so explicitly for citations.py — and citation validation is exactly what discarded 8 of the 16 `present`-stratum answers into the grounded fallback, the single largest lever on the figure Section 3.2.5 quotes (iso25010_evidence.md:79-95).
- **The checkpoint leaks what the published file sanitizes.** sanitize_evaluation_rows() strips rag_context/rag_contexts before writing the JSON and CSV, but _run_pathways writes the RAW row to the checkpoint first (run_comparison.py:521-523). The 1.8 MB pathways.jsonl is full manuscript text. It is saved by the directory-wide gitignore, not by any deliberate control in the harness.
- **The evidence HTML the admin card points at has no generator and no test.** docs/evidence/OBJECTIVE_2_COMPARISON_2026-09-13.html embeds a hand-written `const Q = [...]` of 40 rows and a `const STRATA` table with hand-formatted p-strings ('0.00003', '0.13', '0.042'). I verified all 40 rows against the run JSON — they match to 4 dp, and the notice ids [6,7,14,15,20,32,33,36] match iso25010_evidence.md:80 — but nothing in the repo would catch it if they ever stopped matching.
- **The evidence markdown table disagrees with the artifact it summarizes, knowingly.** iso25010_evidence.md:64-65 prints absent_unreleased 0.1990 -> 0.3600 and absent_by_design 0.2622 -> 0.3761, while the JSON says 0.1991 -> 0.3601 and 0.2616 -> 0.3755. I recomputed both from the per-row Ragas scores: the JSON is right and the md table is a transcription error, left deliberately uncorrected (iso25010_evidence.md:41-46). AdminOverview.jsx formats at 3 dp specifically so the disagreement never reaches the screen (AdminOverview.jsx:28-31).
- **verify.py's most interesting assertion is hardcoded and can go vacuous.** POOLED_P='3.222e-05' and PRESENT_P='0.1257' are literals (verify.py:22-23). The check 'pooled p never without present p' only fires when the pooled string is found; re-run the evaluation, get a different p, and the guard passes trivially while still reporting PASS.
- **The deck labels every notice a 'grounded fallback'.** cmp.notice_rows is every row with rag_kind == 'notice', which by chat_notices.response_kind() also covers no-relevant-thesis, guard refusals, capacity and conversational replies. Slide 9 prints '<n> RAG replies were grounded fallbacks' and charts.py's legend says 'Grounded fallback (N)' (build.py:598, charts.py:129). For this run the 8 happen to all be grounded fallbacks, so the label is accurate by coincidence, not by construction.
- **The harness never touches HTTP.** _chat_impl is called in-process with request=None, user=None (run_comparison.py:271-278), so the measured RAG arm skips SlowAPI rate limiting, Turnstile, the guest token budget middleware and the route wrapper's persistence. The three env settings in the README exist precisely because the one guest control that DOES apply — GUEST_DAILY_TOKEN_BUDGET — would otherwise throttle the run into notices.
- **Line-ending handling is inconsistent across the three writers.** export_objective2_summary.py passes newline=' ' explicitly (:141) and has a test asserting no CR bytes. run_comparison.py (:1014) and release_fingerprint.py (:151) both use bare write_text, so on Windows they emit CRLF. iso25010_evidence.md:118-123 documents this for the results file and records the as-committed LF hashes instead — which I verified: the committed blob is dc01cbbc...2e6bcb1, exactly the recorded value.
- **Dead code in the deck build.** build.py:698 writes `'spins': {str(SLIDES.index(fn) + 1): None for fn in ()}` — a comprehension over an empty tuple, always {}. The real spin data is in ctx.spins and is already applied at :686-689; the sidecar records nothing about it.
- **Two new paper figure builders are orphaned.** paper/Figure_System_Overview.build.py and paper/Figure_System_Architecture.build.py (both untracked) emit SVG, while build_corrections.py's FIGURES list only swaps PNGs for media/image1 and media/image8. paper/figures/figure_ai_pipeline.png is referenced by nothing at all.
- **The chat load evidence is not a concurrency curve and the summarizer cannot say so.** chat_summary_10u.json and chat_summary_20u.json report max_concurrent_threads: 1 despite configuration.users of 10 and 20 — the threads never actually overlapped because every request was returning a 3 ms cooldown notice. jmeter/README.md:78-80 warns the profiles share one depleting quota and must not be presented as a curve, but nothing in summarize_jmeter or summarize_chat_load enforces it.
- **docs/DEFENSE_WALKTHROUGH.md and CLAUDE.md disagree with the evidence the deck reads.** The 2026-09-14 ISO block records 1,201 backend tests passed at 92.07% coverage; CLAUDE.md states the suite 'collects ~2,135 tests'. The deck quotes the evidence file, so slide 9 will say 1,201.
- **A stale absolute claim survives in the evidence file.** iso25010_evidence.md:159 states in bold 'No Objective 2 figure in this repository was measured after this change' (the 2026-09-12 citations.py fix). The formal run is dated 2026-09-13, i.e. after it — the sentence is correct as of its own dated block and false as of the file as a whole.
- **export_objective2_summary drops the split it was written to preserve.** Its docstring argues the export must carry every stratum with its own significance flag rather than a pooled headline, and it does — but for the diagnostics it copies only the pooled faithfulness/context_precision means (:96-99), discarding answers_only (0.7793 / 0.2221) and notices_only (0.2000 / 0.0833). The admin card therefore prints faithfulness 0.663, the figure that mixes 32 answers with 8 fallbacks.

**Adversarial fact-check:** verdict `mostly-accurate`, 11 correction(s), 9 omission(s). Corrections are already folded into the text above. They are kept because each records a wrong belief a competent reader reached from the same files - the same trap is available to you.

- **Wrong:** Step 34 + persistence: "Committed load evidence: provider_summary.json (900 samples, p95 204 ms, 0 errors, 20 users x 5 loops x 3 runs) is the Performance Efficiency figure; rate_summary.json shows 30x200 + 30x429" and "jmeter/*.jtl and *.json — all force-added despite the directory ignore".
  **Actual:** Both files are UNTRACKED. `git ls-files rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/jmeter/` returns only chat-2/5/10/20.jtl, chat-probe.xml, chat_rag_load_report.json and chat_summary_{2,5,10,20}u.json — 11 of the 21 files on disk. provider_run_1-3.jtl, provider_summary.json, rate_run_1.jtl, rate_summary.json, gemini_run_1-3.jtl and gemini_summary.json are all swallowed by the directory ignore. The numbers themselves check out (900 samples, p95 204.05 ms, 0.0% error, configuration users 20 / loops 5 / iterations 3; rate: 60 samples, 200x30 + 429x30) but the single artifact behind the Objective 4 'p95 204 ms' figure — the one assert_quoted forces onto slide 9 — exists only in a developer's working tree plus prose. Note also provider_summary.json reports max_concurrent_threads 2, not 20. (`rag-thesis-backend/.gitignore:23; rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/jmeter/provider_summary.json (untracked); rate_summary.json (untracked)`)
- **Wrong:** Persistence: "Eight other comparison_*.json sit on disk untracked."
  **Actual:** Five. On disk there are 7 comparison_*.json (20260728_140718, 20260901_185026, 20260901_185818, 20260901_191700, 20260903_200643, 20260912_124741, 20260913_064053); three files are tracked (20260728_140718.json, 20260913_064053.json, 20260913_064053.csv), leaving 5 untracked JSON (and 5 untracked CSV). The export script's own comment overcounts in the other direction, calling them "seven earlier exploratory comparisons" when six earlier ones exist. (`rag-thesis-backend/scripts/export_objective2_summary.py:34-36`)
- **Wrong:** Step 29: the test "re-runs build() and asserts byte-equality with the committed file, LF-only bytes, ..."
  **Actual:** It asserts parsed-dict equality (`assert committed == build()`), not byte equality; the only byte-level assertion is the separate LF/trailing-newline test. Far more consequential: the whole module is gated by `pytestmark = pytest.mark.skipif(not SOURCE.exists() or not OUT.exists())`. `rag-thesis-frontend/src/data/objective2Summary.json` is untracked (and NOT gitignored — `git check-ignore` reports nothing), so on a fresh clone OUT is absent, the pin silently skips, and `AdminOverview.jsx`'s module-scope `import OBJECTIVE2 from '../../data/objective2Summary.json'` breaks the Vite build, the unit suite and the e2e build outright. The trace records the untracked state but not that it voids the guard it describes. (`rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_objective2_summary_export.py:30-33, :47-57; rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/admin/AdminOverview.jsx:24`)
- **Wrong:** Step 26: "build() rounds every figure to 4 dp".
  **Actual:** `p_value` is copied raw and `n` is untouched; only baseline/rag/delta/cohens_d_z/ci_95 pass through `_round`. The committed file therefore carries full-precision floats 3.2221579055837075e-05 (pooled) and 0.1256796850888262 (present). This is load-bearing: it is what lets the test assert p≈3.222e-05 and what AdminOverview's `fmtP` (exponential below 0.001) formats. (`rag-thesis-backend/scripts/export_objective2_summary.py:57-58, :72; rag-thesis-frontend/src/data/objective2Summary.json:29, :45`)
- **Wrong:** Step 46 anchor "paper/build_corrections.py:842-876" for build(); step 49 anchor ":877-881" for replace_parts.
  **Actual:** `def build(verbose=True)` is at 837 and `return xml` at 869; 842 is the first `replace_cell_nth` and 876 is already inside the `__main__` block. `D.replace_parts(SRC, DST, ...)` is at 879-880. The described content of build() (SRC read fresh, 13 CELLS at expect=1, two replace_cell_nth, twelve PROSE lists, TABLE_ROWS+TABLE_ROWS9, the Table 2 row move, REFERENCES9, DROP_SPACERS, PAGE_BREAKS) is all correct — CELLS really does hold exactly 13 entries and PROSE..PROSE12 really is twelve lists. (`paper/build_corrections.py:837-869, :879-880, :18-33`)
- **Wrong:** Branch: "--skip-ragas: ... (run_comparison.py:964)".
  **Actual:** The guard is `if not args.skip_ragas:` at line 960. The branch's substance is right — with it set, no `ragas`/`statistics`/`means`/`by_corpus_coverage`/`rag_diagnostics` keys are written, so both `export_objective2_summary.build()` (which indexes run['means'], run['statistics'], run['by_corpus_coverage'], run['rag_diagnostics']) and `deck/evidence.load_comparison()` raise KeyError. (`rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:960`)
- **Wrong:** Surprise anchors in iso25010_evidence.md: transcription note at ":41-46"; the disagreeing rows at ":64-65"; the stale absolute claim at ":159".
  **Actual:** The note is at 35-40 ("One reading the deck build exposed, left uncorrected in the 2026-09-13 block..."); the two rows are at 63-64; the bold sentence spans 153-154 ("**No Objective 2 figure in / this repository was measured after this change.**"). The substance of all three is confirmed verbatim, including that the md prints 0.1990->0.3600 / 0.2622->0.3761 against the JSON's 0.1991->0.3601 / 0.2616->0.3755, and that the file says so itself. (`rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md:35-40, :63-64, :153-154`)
- **Wrong:** latencyNotes: "The deck build downloads six TTFs on first run".
  **Actual:** Seven. FAMILIES is Montserrat (700, 900) + DM Sans (400, 500, 600) + JetBrains Mono (400, 500) = 7 files, fetched only for weights missing from tmp/deck/fonts (so a warm cache makes no network call at all). (`docs/deck/fonts.py:19-23, :38-47`)
- **Wrong:** Surprise: the chunk-size numbers "appear ONLY in a Python comment" and "A reader cannot verify the claim from a clone."
  **Actual:** Overstated. `rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/chunk_size_sweep.py` IS tracked and committed — only its output and the tmp/ embedding cache are not. In the working tree the figures also exist in the uncommitted JSON, and they reproduce the comment exactly: hit@5 75.0/75.0/68.75/68.75 (range 68.75-75.00) and MRR 0.5771/0.6042/0.5333/0.5521 (range 0.533-0.604), with 1200 skipped for "98 of 192 vectors not cached". The honest statement is that a clone cannot verify the claim without re-running the committed sweep against the corpus with embedding quota. (`rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/chunk_size_sweep.py (tracked); rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/results/chunk_size_sweep_2026-09-14.json (untracked); paper/build_corrections.py:123, :129-138`)
- **Wrong:** Step 14: the returns that leave the trace empty include "session-ownership 401", so "the row gets rag_contexts=[] and rag_top_similarity=0.0".
  **Actual:** That path `raise HTTPException(status_code=401, detail='Guest conversations do not have saved sessions.')` — it is a raise, not a return, and it is unreachable from the harness, which constructs `ChatRequest(question=..., department_filter=...)` with no session_id. Had it fired it would propagate into `_attempt_pathways`, be caught by the broad `except Exception`, retried 4x at 20/45/90 s and produce `_unattempted_row` (rag_kind None, a 'failure' string), not a `_build_row` with empty contexts. The conversational/guard fast-path part of the claim is correct, and in the published artifact this branch never fired: all 40 rows carry retrieved_context_count 5. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2113-2121; rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:322-345, :358-377`)
- **Wrong:** Step 12: "no guest-budget middleware".
  **Actual:** Literally true and easy to misread: there is no such middleware because the guest budget lives INSIDE the measured path — `if not user and await asyncio.to_thread(guest_budget.is_exhausted): return _guest_budget_response(...)` plus three further charge points — so it does apply to the harness. `is_exhausted()` returns False only because GUEST_DAILY_TOKEN_BUDGET=0 disables the feature, which is exactly why the README demands it. The trace's surprise #11 states this correctly; step 12 reads as though the control were bypassed. (`rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2218-2223, :2542, :2585, :2691; rag-thesis-backend/services/guest_budget.py:123-127`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- `request` is never dereferenced anywhere in `_chat_impl_unstamped` — it appears only in the signature and in two comments (grep over 2098-2906 returns lines 2100, 2107, 2675 and nothing else). The only real consumers, `ensure_guest_chat_verification(request, user)` and `_client_gone(request)`, are in the route wrapper. So the harness's `request=None` is inert by construction, not by luck — a stronger version of step 12's claim. (rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2016, :2033, :2098-2107)
- The route wrapper also owns the `rag.chat.total` LangSmith span and the pre-resolved `resolve_effective_department` threading. Calling `_chat_impl` directly means the harness's traces start at `rag.retrieval`, and the department is resolved a second time inside `_chat_impl_unstamped` — the code comments there exist specifically to keep the harness working. (rag-thesis-backend/routers/chat.py:2011-2031, :2104-2114)
- `reproducibility` publishes three more digests the trace never lists: `golden_dataset_sha256` (full, the run-id source), `evaluation_requirements_sha256` and `evaluation_script_sha256` (the harness self-hash). And `<run-id>.provenance.json` is a second persisted manifest (the 9-key answering configuration), so "the only place a manifest is actually persisted is inside the comparison JSON" is not strictly true — it is the only place a FULL manifest is persisted. (rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/run_comparison.py:940-948)
- `load_iso_block` raises only for the three `grab()` rows (backend, pylint, frontend). ESLint cleanliness and the Playwright count are plain `re.search`: absent, they degrade silently to `eslint_clean=False` / `playwright_passed=None`, and slide 9 then renders `[VERIFY]` into two card fields with no warning — which `verify.py`'s `no [VERIFY] markers` check fails rather than the build. So step 40's "A missing figure raises ValueError" holds for three of five parsed figures. (docs/deck/evidence.py:217-221; docs/deck/build.py:576-586; docs/deck/verify.py:100)
- `promote()` also copies `archive_state.json` into the committed assets, but only when `--from` starts with `live`. Promoting a fixture set therefore leaves the previous live archive counts (16 papers / 4 tracks / 2026-09-14) standing on slide 7 unchanged and undated. (docs/deck/capture.mjs:322-328)
- `summarize_jmeter` takes `--profile`, `--users`, `--loops` and `--ramp-seconds` as REQUIRED operator-supplied flags, so the `configuration` block in provider_summary.json and the chat summaries is asserted, never derived from the .jtl. The only measured concurrency is `max_concurrent_threads` from `allThreads` — 2 for the provider run and 1 for every chat run, which is the number that actually refutes reading the chat files as a curve. (rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/summarize_jmeter.py:55-57, :88-92)
- Step 45's list of verify.py assertions omits two: `pictures resolve` (every picture's r:embed resolves to a non-empty blob, else 'unresolved'/'empty blob') and, without `--draft`, `no [CAPTURE] placeholders` / `no [VERIFY] markers`. MIN_PT is confirmed to be 14. (docs/deck/verify.py:74-83, :99-100; docs/deck/theme.py:52)
- Verified by computation, which strengthens rather than refutes the trace: sha256(golden_dataset.json) = 5e8fb7f21db6be3c… so run_id 5e8fb7f21db6 is the digest prefix; the committed git blob of comparison_20260913_064053.json hashes to dc01cbbc…2e6bcb1, exactly the value at iso25010_evidence.md:116; and all 40 `const Q` rows in docs/evidence/OBJECTIVE_2_COMPARISON_2026-09-13.html match the run's per-row baseline/rag/faithfulness/context_precision, coverage and kind to 4 dp, with notice ids [6,7,14,15,20,32,33,36] and `const STRATA` p-strings '0.00003'/'0.13'/'0.0009'/'0.042'/'0.012' hand-formatted. rag_diagnostics answers_only/notices_only are 0.7793/0.2221 and 0.2000/0.0833 with not_applicable 0 for both metrics, and every row has retrieved_context_count 5.
- Everything else I checked held: steps 1-13, 15-23, 25, 27-28, 30-33, 35-45, 47-48, 50, all thirteen branches and all nineteen failure modes match the code, including exact strings (`expected {expect}, matched {total}` at paper/_docxlib.py:212-213 and `expected {expect} cell(s), matched {n}` at :115-116), the dead `'spins': {... for fn in ()}` at docs/deck/build.py:698, `POOLED_P`/`PRESENT_P` as literals at docs/deck/verify.py:22-23, the fixture-mode `archive`/`novelty` manifest entries that kill a default deck build, the 11 hashed fingerprint inputs and schema_version 4 as published in the run, and zero references to any of the five commands in .github/workflows/quality.yml (the directory contains only that one file).
